# Preface

The rapid advancement of technology is reshaping the healthcare and pharmaceutical industries in unforeseen manners. The combination of blockchain, big data, and artificial intelligence (AI) has created new opportunities in drug research, clinical trials, and patient care. The book “Digital Blockchain- Big Data, Artificial Intelligence and Virtual Reality in Healthcare Ecosystem” explores the revolutionary potential of these technologies in the pharmaceutical and healthcare industries. Blockchain ensures data integrity, confidentiality, and transparency, whereas big data and AI allow for new advances in drug development, clinical trials, and personalized medicine. Virtual reality is being more widely used in medical training, patient rehabilitation, and treatments, producing immersive environments that improve patient outcomes and healthcare professional capacities.

Beginning with the basis of artificial intelligence (AI) and big data in pharmaceutical research, the book educates readers through the complex relations between these technologies and their uses in healthcare. It investigates blockchain’s rising importance in pharmaceutical supply chain security, as well as the advancements of AI-powered decision-making systems. These digital technologies have a significant impact on the entire lifecycle of medication research and delivery, as well as ethical, regulatory, and intellectual property considerations.

The future of healthcare depends on developing collaborations among pharmaceutical companies, startups, and academic institutions to fully realize the possibilities of AI, big data, and blockchain. This book provides an orientation for healthcare practitioners, technicians, industrial experts, and academicians, as a forward-thinking resource with incorporates disruptive technology into the healthcare ecosystem.

# Foreword

The healthcare sector is undergoing an evolutionary change as a result of the widespread adoption of digital technologies such as blockchain, big data, artificial intelligence (AI), and virtual reality. These technologies not only enhance pharmaceutical research, drug development, and clinical trials, but they also transform patient care, safety, and data management. The book, *Digital Blockchain: Big Data, Artificial Intelligence and Virtual Reality in Healthcare Ecosystem*, authored by Dr. Rishabha Malviya and his team, explores various applications of these technologies in the healthcare and pharmaceutical sector.

The pharmaceutical sector has long played an important role in global healthcare systems, but issues such as difficulties in drug development research, data management complications, and regulatory compliance remain. Blockchain and AI are tackling these concerns by increasing transparency and accelerating drug discovery. The emergence of technologies opens up new possibilities for better, more efficient healthcare systems.

Each chapter provides new insights into how blockchain’s transparency and security, AI’s computing power, and big data’s analytical skills are transforming every stage of the healthcare value chain. This book thoroughly covers the ground to demonstrate the way technology is not only enhancing but also significantly altering every aspect of healthcare, whether it be smart pharma, drug production, clinical trials, or post-market surveillance.

The book emphasizes the value of collaboration among industry stakeholders, including research institutions, pharmaceutical corporations, technological entrepreneurs, and regulatory organizations. The combination of these organizations promises a future in which patient outcomes improve, systems are more transparent, and healthcare is more personalized and accessible.

The content is technically advanced and forward-thinking, taking readers through current developments while forecasting future trends. As AI, blockchain, big data, and Virtual reality merge to form the emerging standard in healthcare, this book is an essential resource for healthcare practitioners, technologists, industrial experts, and researchers.

Happy Reading and Best Wishes.

Greater Noida, India

# Chapter 1 Introduction to smart pharma: foundation of AI and big data

#### Abstract

Due to its potential to completely transform the pharmaceutical business, artificial intelligence (AI) has emerged as a potent instrument in the medical community. However, the potential isn’t fully realized yet. This chapter provides information about AI and big data in the pharma and other healthcare industries. Nevertheless, AI techniques such as big data and natural language processing (NLP) can expedite and rehabilitate the process by facilitating a more precise and efficient analysis of large datasets. To achieve this, the test administration may be simplified. The application of AI in the field of chemistry has recently garnered significant attention due to its potential to provide valuable insights into developing efficient drug delivery systems. AI significantly influences the medication manufacturing process and the drug repurposing strategy. Despite its numerous obstacles, AI has the potential to enhance medical imaging services and help diagnose patients. However, healthcare systems are responsible for informing patients about the benefits and drawbacks of AI-supported healthcare systems. A conclusion of the recent state of the field for AI applications in the healthcare industry is provided in the chapter, taking into account the rapid advancements in AI combined with AI-integrated medical systems.

Keywords:  pharma,  AI,  big data,  deep learning,  machine learning,  NLP,  digital health,  

## 1.1 Introduction

The pharmaceutical industry has been significantly impacted by the recent surge in interest in the application of AI in medicinal chemistry. The objective of the lengthy and complex drug discovery process, which has historically been reliant on resource-intensive methods such as high-throughput screening and experimental testing, is to identify and develop new pharmaceuticals. However, by enabling more precise and effective analysis of big datasets, ML and NLP (natural language processing) methods in AI offer an opportunity to speed up and improve the process. Researchers have recently shown that deep learning (DL) may be successfully applied to precisely forecast the efficacy of medicinal molecules. In recent years, there has been a significant increase in interest in the implementation of AI in the field of medicine, as it has the potential to revolutionize the pharmaceutical industry. The long and intricate drug discovery process has historically relied on resource-intensive techniques such as high-throughput screening and trial-and-error testing to identify and propagate novel pharmaceuticals. Therefore, by enabling more precise and effective analysis of big datasets, ML along with NLP techniques in AI provides a way to speed up and improve the process. Researchers have recently shown that DL may be successfully applied to precisely forecast the efficacy of medicinal molecules \[[1](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_001), [2](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_002), [3](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_003)\]. Medicinal chemistry is currently characterized by the use of a trial-and-error approach and intensive screening procedures. One of these techniques is to analyze an abundance of possible therapeutic molecules to find those that meet the requirements. It has been observed, nevertheless, that these methods are costly, time-consuming, and frequently produce less accurate results. Furthermore, the scope of their application may be restricted by the availability of appropriate chemicals as well as the challenges associated with accurately predicting their physiological reactions. Numerous AI algorithms, such as unsupervised learning strategies, reinforcement learning, and evolution or rule-based algorithms, may be of great assistance in solving these issues \[[4](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_004), [5](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_005), [6](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_006)\].

AI has brought about significant changes in the area of drug research and development (R&D). It involves researching proteins and small compounds, helping to plan clinical trials, and monitoring things after putting them on the market \[[7](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_007)\]. AI includes various methodological areas, such as information representation, reasoning, finding solutions, and, most importantly, machine learning (ML) as a foundational paradigm. Algorithms used in ML can identify patterns within a specially classified dataset. Deep learning (DL) is a specific subfield of ML that focuses on the application of artificial neural networks (ANNs). The aforementioned elements combine to create a network of linked, highly developed computing units known as “perceptions,” which are designed to resemble actual neurons in the human brain and transmit electrical impulses in a comparable way \[[8](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_008), [9](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_009)\]. NLP algorithms can extract relevant details from unstructured data that can be found in a variety of books, articles, and patents. Additional study of the extracted information may reveal the hidden relationships of knowledge in several papers, hence relieving the workload of researchers who would otherwise need to examine every document \[[10](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_010), [11](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_011)\]. Furthermore, algorithms that are used to generate virtual screens based on their capacity to synthesize workable substances include nearest-neighbor classifiers, random forest (RF), extreme learning machines, support vector machines (SVMs), and deep neural networks (DNNs) \[[12](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_012), [13](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_013)\]. These algorithms also can forecast toxicity and in vivo activity. To provide a complete framework that would aid in the identification and development of treatments in areas like immuno-oncology and cardiovascular illnesses, a group of biopharmaceutical firms has partnered with IT companies \[[14](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_014)\]. Based only on a molecule’s chemical structure, the AI/ML models may also anticipate possible biological interactions of a molecule that hasn’t been tested or synthesized. Scientists in the absorption, distribution, metabolism, and excretion (ADME) sector use AI/ML models to identify small molecules with the best potential for synthesis, testing, and process development based on their ADME profiles \[[15](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_015), [16](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_016)\]. Artificial intelligence (AI) facilitates the best possible operation of medication delivery systems and is a useful tool in the discovery and repurposing of pharmaceuticals. It collects information from several sources, evaluates pharmacokinetic, patient, and molecular data, and contrasts unknown compounds with recognized ones. The accuracy with which drug delivery methods are chosen, taking patient needs and illness circumstances into account, determines the efficacy \[[17](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_017), [18](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_018)\].

AI algorithms can forecast the behavior of drugs on various levels by examining intricate connections between pharmacological properties, formulation components, and physiological parameters. They enable the prediction of the physicochemical characteristics, stability, and in vitro release profile of the medicinal compound, thereby facilitating the development of a more comprehensive understanding of efficient drug delivery systems and medication delivery processes. The methodology previously described is also employed to conduct in vivo-in vitro correlation studies and to enhance the evaluation of in vivo pharmacokinetic parameters and drug distribution. By applying appropriate AI techniques, researchers can identify potential risks and impediments related to drug delivery systems (DDS) early in the development phase, enabling proactive alterations and modifications to reduce risks and enhance the effectiveness of medication. AI and computer modeling help to lessen the likelihood of unexpected outcomes by reducing reliance on costly and time-consuming trial-and-error trials \[[19](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_019), [20](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_020)\]. Even though AI applications in pharmacy seem promising, there are still many unsolved research issues. Long-term effects like medication adherence, ethical concerns about data privacy and potential biases, technical difficulties with integrating data and system maintenance, and patient and chemist usability are a few of these. The satisfaction of patients with the device is another factor. To enhance the understanding of the implications of integrating AI into the pharmacological system, scientists may address these knowledge gaps, develop solutions that optimize its benefits, mitigate its drawbacks, and ensure its ethical use \[[21](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_021)\].

## 1.2 Big data in the pharmaceutical landscape

Healthcare data has been digitized with the help of the growth and development of big data. A new domain called “big data” was introduced because of the data’s rapid expansion over the past few years. Big data analytics and other intelligent healthcare solutions, which also save costs and boost efficiency, are making healthcare more mobile and smarter. By employing predictive analytics to forecast pharmacological outcomes and pinpoint the patients who would benefit most from pharmaceutical treatments, chemists can get a deeper understanding of the dangers and adverse effects of drugs \[[22](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_022), [23](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_023), [24](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_024)\]. Big data’s potential benefits in the medical and health sectors have made it a prominent study topic in recent years. Utilizing big data-driven clinical decision support systems has shown promise in treating ailments like Alzheimer’s disease. Utilizing big data techniques, electronic medical record optimization has also been accomplished. Data about chemical characteristics, pharmacokinetics, clinical behaviors, and drug toxicity have been created in the fields of toxicology, pharmaceutics, and pharmacology. High-throughput screening (HTS) and next-generation sequencing (NGS) have significantly increased data output and correspondingly increased the amount of data \[[25](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_025), [26](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_026)\].

Digital health care will be enabled by big data analysis, which will reduce the requirement for in-person visits, enhance patient outcomes, and guarantee that clinicians have immediate access to all patient medical records. Big data analysis will become a vital tool for healthcare organizations via digital medicine with aggregated real-time data, offering more chances for proactive action and a more comprehensive picture of patients’ circumstances \[[27](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_027), [28](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_028)\]. Data streams are constantly expanding in the modern era as a result of the utilization of electronic medical records, the vast amounts of data transmitted by diverse types of sensors, and the information that patients post on social media. The healthcare sector has always generated vast amounts of data. The medical sector also generates substantial quantities of data, such as genetic information, clinical notes, imaging data, and health-related behavior. Healthcare organizations can assist in disease surveillance, medical decision-making, and public health management by effectively utilizing the data \[[29](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_029)\]. The problem can be addressed with the use of electronic health records (EHRs), which are electronic patient health data compilations in digital format. Increasingly, EHRs are being implemented in poor countries. Through a strong network connection, these records may be exchanged between different healthcare settings. Digitally recorded health records are less likely to include duplicate data as they can be shared, updated, and altered throughout different healthcare systems, reducing the chance of missing any necessary paperwork. Physicians, patients, and hospital management all immediately benefit from EHR initiatives \[[30](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_030)\]. The electronic medical record is a critical element of e-health utilization, as it contains information regarding a patient’s health. An electronic medical record is comprised of legal documents that are generated in hospital contexts. Consequently, these data serve as the electronic health record’s principal information source. The term “e-health” was first coined in the early twenty-first century and denotes the integration of contemporary communication and information technologies into the provision of healthcare services, according to a researcher. Effective management of electronic health and the ability to share medical information over greater geographic distances require a multidisciplinary team with experience in computer science, instrumentation, and communications \[[31](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_031), [32](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_032)\]. In big analytics, it is one of the crucial steps, consisting of five stages: data production, acquisition, preservation, analytics, and visualization [fig 1.1.](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_fig_001) The main source of data production is the Internet, which is followed by data acquisition, which entails gathering, sorting, and exchanging enormous volumes of data. Large infrastructure is used to store data efficiently to guarantee its use in the future. Utilizing ML, computational intelligence, and data mining techniques, data analytics can extract data from vast volumes of data, guaranteeing effective use and improved use in the future \[[33](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_033)\].

![Fig. 1.1: Different stages of big data along with classification.](graphic/converted/b_9783111574288-001_fig_001.png)

Fig. 1.1: Different stages of big data along with classification.

Through the use of massive volumes of data, big data offers clinical trial projects real-time monitoring, enabling organizations to efficiently plan trial stages and manage patient involvement. Big data analytics is very helpful in providing intelligent information to avoid or address potential problems as well as find new opportunities to boost efficiency and save costs. Big data analytics, for instance, is utilized in the rare illness sector to predict the risks associated with new publications, research discoveries, or drug interactions. Big data applies predictive analytics to procedures like segmentation and sales force focusing on pharmaceutical products sales as well as supply chain operations, measures sentiment, aids in marketing and sales decision-making, and analyzes high-frequency product transactions \[[34](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_034), [35](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_035)\]. There has been an increase in the use of portable sensors in the monitoring and diagnosis of various medical conditions, including diabetes and other illnesses. These sensors allow patients to receive accurate assessments and recommendations regarding biological factors found in wound fluids, such as pH, temperature, bacterial substances, and sweat biomarkers. A real-time monitoring method for analyte concentrations in wound fluid is provided by a multitude of sensors that are embedded in bandages or dressings. Inkjet-printed sensors are employed in smart bandages to detect intermittent hemorrhage, pH fluctuations, and air pressure changes at the wound site. Moreover, smart bandage detectors can be linked to microneedle biosensors to detect cancer biomarkers like tyrosinase (TYR) and screen for skin melanoma \[[36](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_036), [37](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_037), [38](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_038), [39](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_039), [40](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_040), [41](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_041), [42](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_042), [43](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_043), [44](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_044), [45](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_045)\]. Furthermore, by enhancing public health assessment and surveillance, predictive analytics and big data can increase public health precision. Therefore, from the standpoint of public health, large-scale data collection is an invaluable tool for epidemiological studies, needs for population health assessments, population-based treatment evaluation, and well-informed policy-making \[[46](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_046), [47](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_047)\]. By assisting in the identification of the most effective ways for treating each patient uniquely, the use of big data analytics for healthcare can support individualized care, which may influence lowering medical costs, improving living standards, and reducing healthcare resource waste. Scientists can use inductive reasoning to uncover patterns in exploratory data that are unrelated to any specific hypothesis by analyzing the data using techniques like data mining. Thus, it becomes feasible to do predictive analysis and real-time analysis, which helps medical professionals start treatments earlier and reduces their risk of morbidity and mortality \[[48](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_048)\].

## 1.3 Integration of big data and AI in pharmaceutical research

The process of finding new drugs is a tribute to biological complexity and human inventiveness in the context of advancements in medicine and pharmaceuticals. Big data and AI are two formidable forces that have emerged as leaders in a few industries, including healthcare and pharmaceuticals, as all continue to advance into the information age \[[49](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_049), [50](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_050)\]. The process of finding new drugs could be significantly changed by combining AI with big data. The combination may provide the solution to several issues that have previously plagued the sector, including accelerating drug pipelines, increasing the precision of treatment outcome forecasts, and optimizing R&D expenditures \[[51](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_051), [52](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_052)\]. The methodologies employed to accelerate the drug development process have also evolved in tandem with the advancement of AI and big data. Medication candidates can now be developed in a more organized, cost-effective manner and at a significantly faster pace with the assistance of the AI method. Computing power and algorithms that utilize current information to provide superior analytics and classification are implemented throughout the drug development process, from the discovery of a potential medication to the pharmaceutical industry’s production process \[[53](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_053), [54](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_054), [55](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_055)\].

Big data, or an enormous collection of extensive data from many domains, is increasingly being used in drug development. To analyze the heterogeneous data – which ranges from clinical to bioassay – advanced computer strategies and processing capacity are required. Big data analysis is moving toward high-performance computers, cloud services, and GPUs due to the expanding volume of big data. The intricacy of the data must be resolved in part by AI, underscoring the necessity of sophisticated computing skills in the pharmaceutical sector \[[56](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_056), [57](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_057)\]. Drug research and discovery depend heavily on multidimensional data, such as target structures, chemical assays, chemical frameworks, and clinical data. There are now more opportunities for the successful use of AI in medication development due to the quick growth of these data sources. Advances in hardware have also boosted data availability and processing capability, opening new modeling methodologies such as large-scale networks. Because biocompatible nanomaterials, a kind of modern nanotechnology, offer flexible and dependable drug delivery systems for systemic disorders like cancer, they have had a substantial influence on drug development. The development of biocompatible nanomaterials, AI, and hardware is opening the door to a more successful and efficient drug discovery method \[[58](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_058), [59](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_059)\]. Molecular dynamic (MD) simulations served as the foundation for early attempts to apply AI to nano simulation for drug development. Later, a similar method was employed to assess the carbon nanotubes’ sensitivity to organic substances \[[60](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_060), [61](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_061)\]. AI algorithms, especially ML models, depend on data. The more data they are given, the more accurate, predictive, and adaptive they become. AI evolved as a tool for large data interpretation, analysis, and application in the drug development industry. Furthermore, big data offers a platform for comprehensive patient analysis. The increasing acceptance of personalized medicine underscores the importance of comprehending the impact of an individual’s genetic composition on their response to medication. The customization of drugs to match individual patient profiles, which improves treatment efficacy and reduces adverse effects, was made feasible by AI-driven analysis of large genetic data \[[62](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_062), [63](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_063), [64](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_064)\]. Big data produced during the drug discovery process and made available through open databases may prove to be very beneficial in lowering drug attrition throughout the pipeline. To determine the preferred order of ranking for drug candidates with specified medicinal actions, conventional machine learning techniques like QSAR modeling can be employed. The technique may be employed as well to weed out substances that are inappropriate or have known side effects. Advances in AI have enabled CADD research to leverage the robust architecture of big data studies, thereby taking the field to new heights. These advancements include the creation of several deep learning algorithms and notable boosts in processing power \[[65](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_065), [66](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_066)\]. An important connection between chemical frameworks and their characteristics and activities can still be made with the help of the effective method of QSAR. When QSAR modeling was first used in drug development, it was based on modest data sets and straightforward linear regression techniques. ML is used in drug research and discovery for several reasons other than QSAR. For example, the numerical outline for chemical pattern-matching investigations was used for de novo drug creation using generative models \[[67](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_067), [68](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_068), [69](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_069), [70](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_070), [71](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_071)\]. These propagative models can produce synthetic molecules with the right characteristics and functionality. As a result, by using ML modeling for a range of data types, including public databases, CADD may produce substantial value in the big data age \[[72](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_072), [73](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_073)\].

## 1.4 Role of stakeholders

Stakeholder analysis, which identifies and ranks stakeholders according to the information obtained, is an essential step in the design of health innovations. Because stakeholder selection criteria in participatory research are sometimes ambiguous, stakeholder results must be taken into account while developing participatory planning procedures. Stakeholder analysis aids in understanding the setting in which new technologies are developed and put into use; it directs the planning process regarding individuals, organizations, or groups involved; and it produces plans that facilitate innovation in development and use while avoiding roadblocks that might impede system integration. Stakeholders are appropriately involved in health innovation planning through the process’s fairness and openness, which is ensured by integrating stakeholder analysis into participatory planning procedures \[[74](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_074), [75](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_075), [76](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_076), [77](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_077), [78](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_078), [79](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_079)\].

The marketing approach known as customer relationship management (CRM) places a strong emphasis on the product’s final user, even speculating that the user may be a pharmaceutical marketing patient. Nevertheless, salespeople in the industry are forming connections with doctors, stockists, and retailers rather than with patients. Physicians issue prescriptions for drugs manufactured by a particular business, but merchants and stockists are an essential part of the supply chain and may increase sales by telling local physicians about promotions or providing alternatives to certain prescriptions. Salespeople never have a conversation with their final customer, the patient, yet the patient still purchases and uses the product. Determining the true participants in the pharmaceutical industry is therefore essential. As [Fig. 1.2](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_fig_002) illustrates, several stakeholders are involved in the formulation as well as execution of a pharmaceutical policy framework. Regulatory agencies, patients, physicians, chemists, insurance companies, academic institutions, professional organizations, non-governmental organizations, nonprofit organizations, and consumer groups with no active participation belong to these stakeholders in addition to state and federal governments (all of which include all ministries like health, trade, commerce, and industry). National and multinational manufacturers of medicines are among them. Governments encompass state authorities, the federal government, and any associated ministries responsible for the manufacturing, importation, exportation, investment, licensing, pricing, research and development, and quality control of pharmaceuticals.

![Fig. 1.2: Different stakeholders in the pharmaceutical industry.](graphic/converted/b_9783111574288-001_fig_002.png)

Fig. 1.2: Different stakeholders in the pharmaceutical industry.

When it comes to creating, planning, implementing, and supervising pharmaceutical policies, they are the key participants and, in a way, the pioneers. When creating new pharmaceutical policies, governments must take the initiative and confer with all pertinent stakeholders before issuing a policy framework. The political will of the government in each nation is a critical factor in the development of effective policies for the safe and efficient utilization, procurement, distribution, and quality control of pharmaceuticals, as well as their equal availability, affordability, and financial risk mitigation \[[80](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_080), [81](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_081)\]. Drug regulators have a crucial role in the development and application of pharmaceutical laws in India, particularly in licensing, marketing, distribution, storage, clinical trials, manufacturing, import, export, and import-export. Drug regulators are not, however, at the forefront of the process in nations like India, where the ministries are directly responsible for creating pharmaceutical policy. Because they are knowledgeable about the difficulties and roadblocks that occur in putting these policies into action, they are crucial to the implementation of robust, thorough, and feasible policy creation. Drug regulators are the key players in India for the creation of comprehensive, practical, and robust regulations since they carry out these policies on the ground and have knowledge of the challenges and barriers that arise in doing so \[[82](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_082)\].

Moreover, producers can make their goods available for consumption by physicians, pharmacists, and patients due to pharmaceutical supply networks and loosely regulated marketplaces. That puts manufacturers in a position to evaluate a drug’s quality and, in turn, its suitability for treating common ailments. They also have the authority to determine drug costs, which lowers the cost of prescriptions for people from lower socioeconomic backgrounds. However, pharmaceutical companies are profitable enterprises whose main objective is to maximize shareholder wealth, as is widely known \[[83](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_083), [84](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_084)\]. A vital conduit between the pharmaceutical industry and prescribers is provided by pharmaceutical traders, such as super-stockists, distributors, transportation and forwarding representatives, retailers, wholesalers, and medical representatives. Due to their commercial interests, they stand to gain a great deal from the advertising of branded medications and can significantly influence prescription practices \[[85](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_085)\]. Patients are the last, but certainly not the least, significant stakeholders in the development of pharmaceutical policies since they are the focus of all other stakeholders. As the last users of medications made by licensed, regulated pharmaceutical businesses, they fall under government regulation. Pharmacists distribute these medications, while physicians prescribe them, and pharmaceutical merchants market, supply, and sell them. They serve as the pivot, providing support for all of the other chain links’ tasks and endeavors. The efficacy of a policy framework is determined by its capacity to alleviate patients’ distress and assist them in managing their sickness. As a result, throughout the formulation process, it is essential to carefully analyze the requirements, desires, and ambitions of patients to build a pharmaceutical strategy that is outcome-oriented and patient-driven \[[86](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_086)\]. Other stakeholder groups include chemists, health care funds, health insurance plans, and pharmaceutical distributors. They should be considered clients in a wider sense, given their influence on prescription decisions. The three main clientele in the pharmaceutical sector are payers, physicians, and patients. Consequently, doctors, hospitals, wholesalers, pharmacies, end users, and patients are the three customer sectors that pharmaceutical firms presently focus on with their sales efforts \[[87](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_087), [88](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_088), [89](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_089)\].

## 1.5 Challenges and conquering strategies in smart pharma: foundation of AI and big data

Despite the prospective benefits of AI in drug discovery, there are also a variety of drawbacks and challenges that must be considered. One of the most significant obstacles is the acquisition of pertinent data. The training of AI-based techniques typically necessitates the use of large data collections. The reliability and accuracy of the results can frequently be influenced by the limited number, variable quality, or low quality of the supplied data \[[90](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_090), [91](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_091), [92](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_092)\]. It offers a substantial amount of data, which is used by models that are trained gradually. Due to its higher cost, the company suffered greatly from the data acquisition model from multiple databases. Furthermore, a substantial amount of trustworthy data is required to guarantee the pharmacological analysis. Another obstacle keeping the biomedical and pharmaceutical industries from fully adopting AI is the shortage of skilled personnel to oversee the AI-based platforms \[[93](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_093)\]. Job losses are the result of the use of AI and ML by large corporations, such as pharmaceutical companies, to supplant their human staff and the lack of financing for small organizations. In an ML-based process, decisions are generated by the input data; however, the paths leading from input to output are not transparent. The input data in CNNs is essentially altered by several intricate layers, which eventually produce unpredictable behavior \[[94](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_094)\]. Furthermore, human error in data interpretation influences machine learning predictions when there is no reasoning behind predictions specific to black-box machine learning models. But logical models and decision trees, like relational machine learning, don’t show the fundamental problem. The dependence on model performance is impacted by human users’ incapacity to understand anticipated outcomes. There are still concerns regarding prediction confidence despite the ML models’ encouraging initial results. A pathological failure in the hypothesis generated by machine learning approaches is more likely when an algorithm tries to seek regimes outside of its application scope \[[95](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_095)\]. When AI-based solutions are utilized to create innovative medications, ethical problems arise since prejudice and fairness issues may result. Inaccurate data can affect the reliability and accuracy of findings, thus human researchers need to make sure AI is used fairly and ethically. AI is limited to making predictions based on the facts at its disposal; human researchers are still required to confirm and explain the results. As a result, it is imperative to guarantee that AI is applied equitably and morally while creating novel pharmaceuticals \[[96](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_096), [97](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_097), [98](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_098), [99](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_099), [100](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_100)\]. Millions of chemicals are available to pharmaceutical companies in their data sets for the development of new medications; it may be too much for conventional machine-learning technologies to handle. A computerized model that utilizes the quantitative structure-activity relationship (QSAR) can swiftly ascertain a significant number of compounds or critical physicochemical properties, such as log P or log D. Consequently, these models are unable to accurately anticipate the efficacy and side effects of specific substances, which are complex biological characteristics. Additionally, QSAR-based models are limited by the absence of experimental validations, experimental data errors in training sets, and the brief length of their training sets \[[101](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_101)\]. AI has demonstrated its exceptional utility in the improvement of medical imaging services by its capacity to manage vast quantities of radiology data. AI-based software has the potential to enhance the precision of patient diagnoses, thereby alleviating the burden on medical personnel and simplifying their responsibilities. AI-supported healthcare programs have the potential to improve intelligence as more data is collected and medical research is released. Patients who are not acquainted with AI may be convinced to participate in system-supported operations if they are apprised of the potential for more rapid, precise diagnosis, fewer errors, and reduced medical expenses. Healthcare systems should provide clients with information regarding the aids and disadvantages of AI-integrated medical systems \[[102](#9783111573861_000727.xhtml#b_9783111574288-001_ref_102)\].

## 1.6 Conclusion

AI has transformed the pharmaceutical sector, leading to a significant enhancement in the efficiency of medication discovery and research. The application of AI methodologies, such as machine learning and natural language processing, facilitates the examination of large datasets, the forecasting of medicinal compound effectiveness, and the creation of optimized drug delivery systems. The landscape of the pharmaceutical industry has also been impacted by big data, which has enabled healthcare systems to become more mobile and efficient. AI has the potential to enable personalized medicine, which would result in improved living standards, decreased resource waste, and reduced medical costs. However, there are still obstacles to overcome, such as the difficulty of locating relevant data, the lack of available trained personnel, and ethical concerns. Healthcare systems must provide patients with information regarding the advantages and disadvantages of AI-supported healthcare systems. AI can significantly improve medical imaging services and patient diagnostics, notwithstanding these challenges.
