# Chapter 2 Emerging technology in pharma and the role of AI and blockchain

#### Abstract

The study, development, manufacture, and distribution of novel chemical entities (NCEs) and biological products intended to enhance human health constitute the core activities of the pharmaceutical industry, which follows a methodical and intricate business model. The chapter provides information about the innovative technologies introduced in pharma. In particular, drug discovery and regulatory approval are two areas where the pharmaceutical industry could undergo a radical transformation because of AI. Blockchain technology may significantly enhance the safeguarding of data confidentiality and the verification of intellectual property asset generation. Blockchain technology is also applicable to medical consultation frameworks, protecting users’ privacy and security, an essential consideration from the perspective of the patient when it comes to psychological and sexual diseases. Nonetheless, there are several obstacles that blockchain technology must overcome, including data management, privacy issues, data redundancy, and integration with current healthcare systems. An overview of the latest developments in artificial intelligence and blockchain technology is provided in the chapter.

Keywords:  blockchain,  pharmaceuticals,  ELN,  LifeCrypter,  GCoin,  electronic health record data,  

## 2.1 Introduction

The business model of the pharmaceutical sector is intricate and focused, with a primary emphasis on the production, marketing, research, and development of new chemical entities (NCEs) and biological products that are designed to improve human health \[[1](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_001)\]. In the pharmaceutical industry, “advanced analytics” are utilized throughout the entire value chain, including development and research, safety, production, as well as regulation. These analytics are developed from various industries \[[2](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_002)\]. The most important component of the healthcare system is the pharmaceutical sector, which is in charge of manufacturing and distributing drugs, biological substances, and medical equipment used in disease diagnosis and treatment. Additionally, the pharmaceutical industry engages in research to create new products that are beneficial to human welfare. Because numerous pharmaceutical goods are lifesaving, maintaining the final products’ quality is crucial to avoiding any possible health hazards \[[3](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_003)\]. A straightforward prerequisite for the achievement of the international organization’s objectives for sustainable development is the establishment of a pharmaceutical manufacturing unit. Encouraging and improving the capabilities of the populace is vital to controlling their wellbeing and producing novel drugs that are both more affordable and effective. It will help achieve one of the main aims of sustainability, which is the reduction of poverty \[[4](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_004), [5](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_005)\].

“Traditional technology” refers to a three-step energy conversion process that was in use before the fourth industrial revolution. The needs of patients in both armed forces and civilian populations, along with healthcare providers, are not sufficiently met by the traditional manufacturing of pharmaceutical products. Traditional manufacturing is inadequately qualified to oversee and manage complex, computerized manufacturing processes for the proficient and profitable production of customized items. The capabilities of conventional technologies for the development of materials, including those used in medical applications, have reached their limits \[[6](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_006)\]. It is possible to eliminate prescription errors that are caused by selecting the incorrect options from a selection box with the assistance of AI, which plays a significant role in the process as shown in [Fig. 2.1](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_fig_001). Artificial intelligence (AI) can recognize errors like these by analyzing the patient’s medical records. Lowering the chance of drug overdose along with additional health risks contributes to an improvement in the standard of care. Human error in the diagnosis of CT and MRI scans is reduced by AI, which improves the precision of treatment. It mitigates the danger to patient wellbeing, protects the hospital’s reputation, and decreases costs related to the breach of sensitive patient information \[[7](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_007)\]. In recent decades, technology has significantly transformed every aspect of human life, including transportation, communication, manufacturing, business, and the pharmaceutical and medical sectors. The pharmaceutical industry has begun to shift away from traditional careers and toward more-advanced careers to facilitate the production of medicines for the next generation. The variety of work available in the pharmaceutical research industry is expanding as career opportunities continue to improve. As a result of technological advancements, cutting-edge career opportunities are emerging. Through innovative research, these possibilities have been shown to greatly boost the pharmaceutical industry’s output and introduce the next generation of medications onto the market \[[8](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_008), [9](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_009)\].

![Fig. 2.1: Comparison of traditional treatment by healthcare professionals with AI-assisted healthcare professionals.](graphic/converted/b_9783111574288-002_fig_001.png)

Fig. 2.1: Comparison of traditional treatment by healthcare professionals with AI-assisted healthcare professionals.

A growing number of institutions, companies, and scholars are becoming interested in blockchain technology, especially when it comes to using Bitcoin, a type of virtual currency. A diverse array of sectors and industries, such as aerospace, automotive, computing and electronics, engineering, business and accounting, banking and finance, defense, and healthcare, have been transformed by the application of intelligent technologies other than blockchain. These technologies include AI, virtual reality, the Internet of Things (IoT), and machine learning \[[10](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_010), [11](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_011), [12](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_012), [13](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_013), [14](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_014)\]. There is a novel, integrated, one-of-a-kind, and innovative computing technology that are a part of blockchain technology. The system includes transparent transactions, sophisticated consensus-building processes, dynamic public key algorithm encryption, programmable smart contracts, decentralized peer-to-peer networking, and decentralized retention \[[15](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_015)\]. One of the numerous benefits of blockchain technology is that the contracts and transactions that are incorporated into the distributed database are impervious to modification. Decentralization, transparency, trust, privacy, and stability are among the numerous attributes of blockchain technology. A viable approach to preventing the sale of counterfeit medications has been identified through the implementation of blockchain technology in the pharmaceutical industry. Blockchain technology enables the monitoring of the procurement of raw materials, the distribution of medications, and the origin of pharmaceuticals. Furthermore, the implementation of blockchain technology leads to a decrease in the number of intermediaries, which in turn improves the safety ratings and reduces the costs of the pharmaceutical method \[[16](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_016), [17](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_017)\].

The supply chain system as a whole is equipped with verification processes that validate the product serial numbers within the parameters of the following technology. Additionally, the traceability of drugs has been improved to prevent theft, and quality control has been accomplished through the utilization of digital signatures. In addition, to guarantee that the quality remains consistent, blockchain chain codes, medical records, and data mining have been instituted from the supplier to the pharmacy \[[18](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_018)\]. Furthermore, delivery delays can hurt business operations as a whole, and in the pharmaceutical and medical sectors, they can result in fatalities or the exacerbation of maladies. The pharmaceutical supply chain has implemented blockchain technology. Drug monitoring and provenance are indispensable for regulatory compliance, patient health, and business operations. Sophisticated and secure monitoring and tracking technologies enable the timely delivery of products in transit to their intended destinations, thereby enabling the pharmaceutical business and patient management. To expedite the global shipment of pharmaceuticals, a secure global registry was established. Even though the technology offers substantial opportunities for large pharmaceutical companies, it is also beneficial for smaller businesses \[[19](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_019), [20](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_020)\].

## 2.2 Role of blockchain in the pharmaceutical industry

Drug discovery, drug progress, as well as regulatory certification, comprise the multi-year, intricate process of pharmaceutical development and research. Blockchain is a decentralized computer network that provides its members with access to a secure ledger of transactions and operates without a central server. By time stamping each transaction made within the network, it eliminates the need for an outside organization. Every network node maintains its local ledger and maintains a local copy of the record. Modest changes made to the localized ledger are reflected across the network. Beyond just cryptocurrencies, blockchain technology enables the creation and monitoring of any sort of digital asset. Within the pharmaceutical supply chain, a product created by a manufacturer and registered on the network can be transferred to other participants like cryptocurrency, as it is considered a digital asset. Once again, blockchain ranks first for privacy protection. Each member of a blockchain network is given a key pair that serves as their identity \[[21](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_021), [22](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_022)\]. The blockchain approach maintains patient privacy and data security while offering a special benefit for patient recruitment. Patient recruitment times are shortened by using blockchain distributed ledgers because anonymously registered patients can be linked to various clinical trial sites. As a result, the researcher can select the necessary subjects from a group of patients by the study plan. Proposals have also been made for a blockchain structure that involves multiple contracts for clinical trials to manage the studies and involve patients \[[23](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_023)\]. Also, the clinical trials are tracked using the blockchain technique, which encourages others to participate in clinical research while also instilling confidence in the outcomes. Because it can result in fewer clinical trials where positive results are covered selectively, transparency is an essential part of drug discovery research. Consequently, applying blockchain technology to clinical trials encourages improved relationships between sponsors and different clinical research sites in addition to enhancing data integrity and transparency \[[24](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_024)\].

Participants in clinical research can also access shared data due to blockchain technology. Even though big data increases CT storage capacity, its vast volumes, variety, and rapidity of records pose several challenges for decentralized storage frameworks \[[25](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_025)\]. A compelling feature of blockchain is its ability to securely share real data with multiple stakeholders, which can simplify the administration of clinical trials. Clinical trials evaluate a novel drug’s effectiveness after it is developed. Clinical trials are drawn-out procedures with intricate interactions between numerous regulatory review boards located in different places and different data sources. Clinical trial data must be collected and shared while maintaining confidentiality and proprietary information. Accurately and effectively managing clinical data from various information sources is difficult, though. By providing a unified platform for sharing information among all parties involved, blockchain technology can enhance the caliber of clinical trial results \[[26](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_026)\]. First, all the data from different sources such as social media, laboratories, medical equipment, and many more is combined to create raw data. Later on, the following data grows into big data. The data that follows is indispensable to the blockchain-based healthcare system and functions as the primary building element for the initial layer of the stack. The fundamental framework for constructing a four-component, secure healthcare architecture is the unprocessed information layer, which is integrated with blockchain technology. Consensus protocols and algorithms are among the features that vary amongst blockchain platforms. Numerous blockchain platforms have been developed to help users generate and oversee their transactions as shown in [Fig. 2.2](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_fig_002) \[[27, 28](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_027)\]. A blockchain-based solution called LifeCrypter has been introduced to improve security and credibility in the world’s pharmaceutical supply chain. By employing clear identification tags, the system can maintain ownership data throughout its trade history, resulting in a reliable, transparent, and traceable drug supply chain network. Another reason the GCoin blockchain was introduced was to offer a transparent, consensus-driven, and unchangeable drug supply chain. To detect counterfeit and diverted medications, a Hyperledger\-based framework is created to ensure patient privacy, security, and transparency \[[29](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_029)\]. Additionally, researchers have used blockchain-based smart contracts to manage, secure, and analyze the sensors in real-time as well as to deal with PHI (protected health information) produced by such sensors. Researchers propose an entirely tier-based design for healthcare, where the ongoing medical monitoring system is managed by a patient-centric agent. To manage real-time data streams, some researchers have also introduced an architecture that involves a patient agent that coordinates blockchain implementation \[[30](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_030), [31](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_031)\]. The demonstration of the creation of intellectual property assets and the protection of data confidentiality can be significantly facilitated by blockchain technology. The blockchain-enabled Electronic Laboratory Note (ELN) is a digital notebook that facilitates the systematic, secure, and efficient sharing and archiving of laboratory data. The system guarantees security and permits the delayed submission of a reduced number of novel medicinal products of legitimate interest. Before transferring the intellectual property documents to the blockchain and creating a record on the ledger, the researchers encrypt them to prevent unauthorized access. The researcher can obtain a blockchain certificate by submitting the encryption key produced during the document upload on the blockchain \[[32](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_032)\]. Furthermore, before a medication is delivered to consumers, it may travel thousands of kilometers from its production facility. Logistics firms must keep an eye on the medication as it moves throughout the supply chain to make sure it is handled, transported, and stored in the best possible way. The effectiveness of the products may be adversely affected by improper maintenance of the appropriate conditions. Blockchain technology’s decentralized structure, immutability, and transparency ensure that all participating authorities will abide by the rules. Smart contracts can be used to manage any deviations from complying with conditions by notifying the appropriate supply chain stakeholders. Researchers are conducting studies with the use of blockchain to monitor and trace products. Furthermore, researchers are incorporating Internet of Things sensors to monitor optimal temperature and humidity levels throughout the supply chain \[[33](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_033)\].

![Fig. 2.2: Numerous blockchain platforms used in the pharma sector to help users.](graphic/converted/b_9783111574288-002_fig_002.png)

Fig. 2.2: Numerous blockchain platforms used in the pharma sector to help users.

The design, sharing, and preservation of electronic health record data within and between institutions have been the subject of extensive research in the field of blockchain technology. By creating secure online storage for patients’ private medical records, researchers created a secure blockchain structure to facilitate medical data sharing. Within the structure, medical management of data is accomplished through the use of a digital archive with information owners’ access control rights. By using cloud encryption underneath the chain, it is stored. Another researcher used blockchain technology and multiple authorities to create an attribute-based authentication scheme in a related study that guaranteed and validated EHRs. It makes broadcasting group messages easier and may be resistant to collusion attempts. Additionally, some researchers have suggested a secure cloud-based electronic medical record system that is built on blockchain and attribute-based digital currencies. To encrypt medical data, digital signs were employed in conjunction with identity-based encryption and identity-based signatures. In addition to blockchain technology, other methods are implemented to ensure the authenticity and accountability of healthcare facilities \[[34](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_034), [35](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_035)\].

## 2.3 Convergence of AI and blockchain in the pharmaceutical industry

Blockchain is still considered a potentially transformative technology that has the potential to revolutionize how people communicate, conduct financial transactions, monitor markets, and record transactions. The potential of blockchain technology is to eradicate the need for a central authority to supervise and authenticate transactions and agreements between multiple parties. Another evident field that is rapidly gathering traction is AI. AI enables machines to acquire knowledge from data and make decisions based on their acquired knowledge. The idea of decentralized AI has advanced in recent years. Decentralized AI is constructed upon a combination of blockchains and AI. It facilitates the distributed, middleman-free processing and storage of meticulously classified, trustworthy, and shared data on the blockchain. It is now expected that the blockchain will serve as a reliable medium for storing the data, and AI is said to be able to process enormous volumes of data. Smart contracts can be incorporated into blockchain programming, enabling dependable third parties to keep an eye on user data sharing and access \[[36](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_036), [37](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_037), [38](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_038), [39](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_039)\]. When combined with AI, blockchain technology has the potential to improve the supply chain in a variety of ways. The protection of local or regional supply systems and the provision of the requisite intelligence to improve operational efficiency may be achieved through the integration of blockchain technology and AI in a supply chain. It can facilitate decision-making and enhance data security and efficiency. Blockchain safely stores enormous volumes of data, while AI analyzes, deduces, and generates novel situations and patterns from data behavior \[[40](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_040), [41](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_041), [42](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_042)\].

By integrating AI with the blockchain, the supply chain could become fully automated and undergo a fundamental transformation. The AI platform can gather data from a variety of sources, including sales data, point-of-sale information, and past purchases, through integration with the blockchain. It makes it possible to identify the characteristics of the data and apply predictive analysis, which is useful for tasks like route planning, demand forecasting, revenue projections, and network management. The researchers in their study recommended the implementation of blockchain technology and AI to ensure the secure distribution of medications throughout the entire supply chain. A triggered request-response mechanism allows for the transfer of any product in the chain between verified chain members. A product can be traced back to its source through the use of smart contracts, which utilize the blockchain to record each exchange between entities. The experiment results show that the method is both feasible and considerably more secure than existing approaches \[[43](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_043), [44](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_044)\]. Researchers addressed the problem of minor medical evaluations by integrating AI and BC technologies. The use of BC in medical consultation frameworks ensures users’ privacy and security, which is crucial when it comes to psychological and sexual diseases from the patient’s perspective. Both AI and BC techniques have an extensive variety of applications in the medical insurance industry. AI has been used to create personalized offers for patients suffering from chronic illnesses, assist insurance (case) managers in managing insurance claims, and help them assess cases more accurately and decisively \[[45](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_045)\].

Blockchain and AI are two technologies that have each shown promise for advancement in the healthcare industry. The use of these methods in the processing of EHR data is of interest to the scientific community. However, blockchain is starting to appear more frequently in the medical industry. It addresses the problems with current EHR frameworks’ interoperability to prevent malicious misuse and record-altering of patient information. At present, several EHR-based system types, including Epic, employ live AI solutions for population health management and clinical decision-support platforms to predict patient deterioration, mortality rates, hospitalizations, and risk levels. In the same vein, MedRec, a blockchain-based platform, has enabled patients to access information \[[46](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_046), [47](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_047)\]. While blockchain technology can ensure security and integrity, adding AI capabilities could be advantageous for the health sector. At least as effective as humans, AI is currently primarily used to identify anomalies in CT and X-ray scans, a task that ensures more individualized treatment. Due to the significant advantages that could result from reducing medical costs and raising the standard of care, experts predict that significant inventions will surface in the future \[[48](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_048)\]. Therefore, the integration of state-of-the-art AI tools with blockchain technology will facilitate the decentralization and acceleration of biomedical studies and healthcare. It was demonstrated that deep learning (DL) is a potent AI technique that has been effectively employed in a variety of disciplines that have been the subject of research by certain researchers, such as drug classification, chemical synthesis, medical diagnostics, and cell image analysis. Additionally, it was demonstrated how DL has shown to be a useful tool for drug discovery and research \[[49](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_049)\]. The goal of personalized cardiovascular medicine could be advanced by integrating blockchain technology with AI. Additionally, by sharing proprietary AI algorithms for generalization, decentralizing vendor or health system databases, providing incentives for solutions that perform better than those that do not, and greatly increasing the availability of data for AI development and training, other researchers studying the combination have discovered ways to accelerate it. These applications remain in their infancy, and there are some concerns about how they should be used. Further study is required in areas like technological trust, ethical considerations, compensation, and whether competing businesses would approve of the data flow in practice \[[50](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_050)\].

Among other possibilities, blockchain-based smart contracts that integrate AI models may suggest the return of expired goods, the restructuring, payment, or buying of stock based on specified limits and events, the resolution of disagreements, and the selection of the most environmentally friendly shipping option. The impact of AI and blockchain technology on an automation service is examined by the researchers. The investigator opted for a secondary data collection approach to obtain pertinent data and details regarding the topic of the study \[[51](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_051)\]. Blockchain also contributes to the advancement of AI by facilitating access to immense amounts of data from internal and external sources, enhancing control over data use and model sharing, generating more actionable insights, and promoting a more dependable and transparent information market. Using outside organizations, or oracles, to process data, researchers proposed a framework that leverages the use of blockchain as a ledger and smart contract platform for controlling robots. Researchers demonstrated how to use smart contracts to regulate robots, securely record events, and interface with external AI systems for the analysis of images \[[52](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_052)\]. Data on blockchain is extremely safe because encryption is integrated right in. Storing sensitive and confidential data, such as health records or personal recommendations, on a blockchain seems logical. Massive, ongoing data sets are necessary for AI. Research and development efforts are currently concentrated on developing AI algorithms that can securely handle encrypted data.

## 2.4 Regulatory considerations of AI in pharma

Although its application in pharmaceutical regulatory affairs is still in its infancy, AI holds great promise for revolutionizing the sector. AI can help with regulatory affairs by providing information about the latest advancements in the field, particularly the potential benefits of AI for regulatory approval and drug discovery. AI can assist pharmaceutical researchers in expediting and enhancing decision-making by automating processes, identifying novel opportunities, and refining judgment. It facilitates the expedited introduction of novel pharmaceuticals to the market. Regulatory affairs refer to the divisions within pharmaceutical corporations that act as intermediaries with regulatory authorities, both national and worldwide, aiming to get product approval by prevailing rules and regulations. Regulatory bodies approve products based on the information and paperwork submitted during the drug discovery and product development process \[[53](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_053), [54](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_054)\]. Blockchain, internet-based computing, 3-D bioprinting, and AI are examples of consumer technology innovations with applications in the healthcare sector. These developments necessitate new regulations to keep up with the rapidly evolving field of technology \[[55](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_055)\]. The Food and Drug Administration (FDA) of the United States is crucial in advancing the development of innovative technologies by establishing technical standards and guidelines, in addition to defining supplementary objectives to enhance the regulatory approval process. Recent developments in AI algorithms and their broad applicability point to significant consequences for medical technology and healthcare. Artificial general intelligence, or AGI, is defined as AI that demonstrates human-like intelligence and can carry out any intellectual task that a human can. An FDA analysis indicates that artificial narrow intelligence (ANI), a category of AI commonly employed in medical technology, is designed specifically for a singular goal \[[56](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_056), [57](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_057)\].

AI must be regulated immediately because it is beginning to surpass human intelligence, and the widespread use of AI may have unfavorable consequences. The European Commission’s “RoboLaw Project,” which looked into the legal ramifications of robotics, produced the Guidelines on Regulating Robotics. Due to its emerging nature, AI cannot be confined to a single jurisdiction and thus requires global regulation. The risks that AI algorithm developers must deal with include dataset shifting, careless confounder fitting, and inadvertent discriminatory bias. The generalization to new groups and the potential unintentional adverse impacts of new algorithms on health outcomes are additional challenges that call for careful consideration and regulation \[[58](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_058)\]. The hazards that AI algorithm developers have to contend with are inadvertent discriminatory bias, negligent confounder fitting, and dataset shifting. The generalization to new groups and the potential unanticipated negative effects of new algorithms on health outcomes are more concerns that require careful consideration and regulation. The FDA provides opportunities to extract validation information from real-world scenarios as well as release medical products based on it, with no need for setting up specialized clinical studies. Any information about a patient’s health status that can be gathered from sources such as product and disease databases, electronic health records, or patient-generated data gathered from settings used at home is referred to as real-world data. These initiatives demonstrate that the FDA views possibilities for software-based devices as well as patient health as being improved by data-driven strategies and dynamic development processes. AI systems must generally be viewed as dynamic systems. When more data is provided, it is meant to get better \[[59](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_059), [60](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_060)\].

As a result, AI-based devices are meant to be dynamic rather than to have a set status. Medical device regulations, on the other hand, demand that the release be in a fixed status. A thorough validation of the status is required before its release. Every time new data is received, the device would need to be released again. It prevents the database from changing continuously while the application is running. It would be required to validate and release the entire process. In concept, process-oriented validation extends beyond products to other software-based tools used in an organization’s quality system. As a result, when medical devices are evaluated continuously using AI tools as mandated by the MDR – it is also relevant. The FDA’s Pre-Cert program presently implements fundamental measures toward the approach \[[61](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_061)\]. The regulation lays out the following: (i) standard operating procedures for the sale, use, and functioning of AI systems within the Union; (ii) limitations on the application of specific AI techniques; (iii) unique specifications for high-risk AI systems along with operator responsibilities; (iv) standard operating procedures for the application of AI and pharmacovigilance with COVID-19; rules governing transparency for AI systems used to generate or modify image, sound, or video content; emotion recognition and biometric classification systems; and AI systems intended to interact with people; and (v) laws governing market surveillance and monitoring \[[62](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_062)\]. The European Commission is prepared to create a legal framework that focuses on the risks associated with the specific applications of AI systems. Three categories of AI applications are distinguished by the risk-based approach: (1) unacceptable risk, (2) high risk, and (3) moderate to low risk \[[63](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_063)\]. Certain AI systems that pose risks deemed “unacceptable” would be forbidden. Numerous “high-risk” AI systems could get approved, but only after meeting certain conditions and fulfilling certain duties to be allowed to enter the EU market. Very minimal transparency requirements would apply to AI systems that pose only “limited risk.”

The Health Ministry has been working to fulfill the regulatory role in quality management, consolidation, data collection, and consent standard-setting with the NDHB (2019) and the EHR Standards (2016). Enabling the ecosystem to share data and compile it in a way that is suitable for AI will advance it technologically. To facilitate data sharing, the government should do more than just establish plans and standards; instead, it should establish data marketplaces and cooperative initiatives. Since the healthcare industry is still in its infancy, the regulations governing AI are still changing. Additionally, there is a chance to have adaptable regulation and gain knowledge from past mistakes in finding the ideal balance between excessive regulation, which could impede the implementation of meaningful public health initiatives on a large scale, and insufficient regulation, which could jeopardize user trust, safety, effectiveness, and adoption. As a result, the regulator must take into account two things: the clinical assessment of AI models and the post-market surveillance and tracking of AI models that are being used \[[65](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_065)\].

## 2.5 Challenges of blockchain in pharma

The domain of blockchain technology research is expanding. Initially confined to the banking industry, blockchain technology has now been adopted by government agencies, the Internet of Things, reputational frameworks, and security services. The pharmaceutical business has lately implemented blockchain technology, mostly to address the issue of counterfeiting. Blockchain technology has two primary advantages due to its decentralization: information security and privacy. Nonetheless, it necessitates increased processing power. About fifty percent of the energy is allocated to thwarting hostile user attacks; without efficient tracking and tracing, users may suffer losses due to theft and misplaced items \[[66](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_066)\]. Global secure banking transactions can be facilitated by blockchain technology, but interoperability concerns pose a serious challenge. Distributed ledger technology can be used to standardize blockchain operations (DLT). Further research is necessary to ascertain the best approaches to implement DLT applications, even as the International Telecommunication Union (ITU) strives to identify and standardize DLT applications and their services. The utilization of edge computing, cloud computing, and advanced Internet of Things applications has markedly enhanced data accessibility \[[67](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_067)\]. The enormous growth presents many challenges for the implementation of blockchain, including data management, privacy issues, redundancy issues, and accessibility issues. Additionally, because of misbehavior and poorly run supply chains, it has tried to leverage blockchain technology to speed up medical transactions, safeguard patients, and track and trace \[68, 69\]. Ensuring interoperability between diverse healthcare equipment and systems is also crucial. For smooth exchange of information and interaction between the various hospitals and systems, robust interoperability solutions are required. The blockchain network must accommodate increased transaction rates without incurring performance degradation as healthcare data volume expands. To ensure blockchain implementations adhere to rigorous data protection requirements such as GDPR and HIPAA, meticulous design and enforcement protocols are essential \[[68](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_068), [69](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_069), [70](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_070), [71](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_071), [72](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_072)\].

Researchers assert that the integration of blockchain technology with the existing healthcare system presents significant challenges. Since numerous hospitals still use legacy systems, careful planning is required to guarantee backward compatibility and a smooth transition. Furthermore, it is imperative to tackle the energy consumption issues associated with blockchain technology, specifically with proof-of-work consensus. Blockchain solutions that are energy-efficient and environmentally friendly should be taken into consideration by healthcare organizations \[[73](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_073), [74](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_074), [75](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_075), [76](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_076), [77](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_077)\]. Also, managing storage capacity is another challenge. Blockchain, engineered to document and manage transactional data, possesses a constrained scope, hence necessitating minimal storage capacity. As technology evolved and penetrated the healthcare sector, storage challenges emerged. Daily, substantial data from the healthcare sector need processing. In a blockchain context, all data, such as patient records, medical histories, test results, MRI scans, X-rays, and other images, will be available to every node in the network, requiring substantial storage capacity. As blockchain technology advances, it encounters not only the previously noted technological issues but also social obstacles, including cultural transformations. Embracing and implementing a technology that is entirely disconnected from conventional work methodologies is invariably challenging. The medical profession is progressively transitioning to digitalization; however, substantial advancements are required before it completely adopts technology, particularly blockchain, which now lacks clinical confirmation. To get doctors to rely on technology rather than paperwork, it will take time and work. Because the healthcare industry has been slow to adopt new technology and policies, the policies offered are not widely trusted \[[78](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_078), [79](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_079), [80](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_080), [81](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_081)\].

Blockchain technology remains in its early stages of development and refinement. The technology will initially cost institutions more and will need to be embraced and integrated over time. It’s also necessary to address issues with non-standardization, scalability, information decay risk, and integration with current legacy systems. A related issue is blockchain transaction verification time. Technical implementation challenges include unproven systems, hidden operating costs, and the need for a network of connected computers (nodes) to process transactions and create blocks \[[82](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_082), [83](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_083)\]. No incentives exist to use blockchain technology to build a vast network of interconnected information “blocks” to replace national record-keeping. Blockchain could improve the current system but not replace it. The majority of medical data would not be stored on the blockchain. To describe a particular patient or procedure, a small amount of information would be retained in each “block” \[[84](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_084)\]. In some scenarios, the growing volume of daily transactions causes the blockchain to get heavy, which causes scalability problems. It makes developing blockchain-based real-time healthcare applications difficult because they cannot process millions of medical records. Furthermore, because of the limited block capacity at the moment, miners may choose to verify transactions with higher transaction rewards before completing smaller ones. On the other hand, large-size blocks decrease transaction speed. Blockchain networks necessitate security as users generate addresses rather than utilizing their actual identities. Due to the visibility of each private key’s balance and activities to all network participants, blockchain-based solutions are unable to provide transaction confidentiality. In the medical field, safeguarding sensitive patient data is essential, which makes the issue crucial \[[85](#9783111573861_002146.xhtml#b_9783111574288-002_ref_085), 86\]. As a result, blockchain technology is still developing and addressing social issues like a culture shift. Technology that differs from traditional work methods is hard to accept. Blockchain technology is gradually making its way into the pharmaceutical industry, but full adoption of the technology takes time. Cultural resistance may occur because most patients dislike having their data shared. To use blockchain in the framework, some people must change their behavior to accept distributed data sharing \[87\].

## 2.6 Conclusion

Healthcare relies on the pharmaceutical industry, which researches, develops, manufactures, and markets drugs, biological compounds, and medical devices. Traditional manufacturing methods have reached their limits, and AI can help eliminate prescription errors and improve care. Blockchain technology, particularly Bitcoin, has gained interest in the pharmaceutical sector due to its decentralization, transparency, trust, confidentiality, and stability. It has been utilized to avoid counterfeit drugs, track drug origins, improve drug transparency and quality control, and aid the pharmaceutical supply chain. Blockchain allows safe transactions as well as privacy protection, decreasing patient recruitment times and enhancing the precision of data and transparency. Platforms like LifeCrypter, GCoin, Hyperledger, and smart contracts have been developed to improve security and credibility in the pharmaceutical supply chain. Blockchain technology is utilized to create, exchange, and safeguard EHR data, ensuring the validity and accountability of healthcare institutions. AI has a significant potential to transform the pharmaceutical sector, especially in drug discovery and regulatory approval processes. The US Food and Drug Administration (FDA) and the European Commission’s “RoboLaw Project” have provided technical standards and guidelines for AI algorithms. However, blockchain technology faces challenges such as data management, accessibility, redundancy, privacy, and integration with existing healthcare systems.
