# Chapter 4 Education and training in smart pharma

#### Abstract

A “connected health” movement is being sparked by the digitalization of healthcare, which also lowers per capita costs, enhances care quality, and improves patient outcomes. The pharmaceutical industry is expanding at a rapid rate due to digitalization, which uses information shared via social media, mobile phones, and other technologies to comprehend consumer demand and optimize supply chain efficiency. Machine learning algorithms can retrieve drug-drug interactions and predict their effects. Big data analysis enables digital health care by eliminating the need for in-person visits and guaranteeing that clinicians have immediate access to all medical records. The physical networking of software, embedded systems, and electronic sensors that permit data sharing across a network from any location is known as the Internet of Things (IoT). The pharmaceutical industry is using blockchain technology to expedite regulatory approval, drug development, and discovery. The Internet of Things is also being used to monitor health problems, predict events about patients, alter data, reduce expenses, and save lives. The chapter offers state-of-the-art training approaches for the smart pharmaceutical industry.

Keywords:  pharma,  virtual reality,  gamification,  academic,  collaboration,  Internet of Things,  

## 4.1 Introduction

The pharmaceutical sector is among the global economic domains exhibiting the swiftest growth rate. The efficiency of the supply chain is enhanced by digitalization, which involves the exchange of information through mobile devices, system integration, and numerous other channels to comprehend and anticipate consumer demand. In the aftermath of COVID-19, businesses in all sectors are more dependent on digital technology than ever to improve performance by increasing manufacturing productivity, enhancing competitiveness, improving long-term financial viability, and using more accurate forecasting and planning \[[1](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_001)\]. The digital revolution in healthcare is fostering a “connected health” movement that has the potential to benefit all parties involved by enhancing the treatment experience, reducing per capita costs, and improving patient outcomes \[[2](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_002), [3](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_003)\]. For instance, the implementation of digitalization to create pharmaceuticals that are resistant to counterfeiting and have serial numbers that may be traced throughout the supply chain will guarantee quality and ensure adherence to forthcoming serialization regulations \[[4](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_004)\]. Pharmaceutical companies may employ digitalization as an additional tool to meet the anticipated global market demand. They could utilize digitalization to more effectively communicate with distributors and suppliers, comply with regulations, and identify cost-saving production efficiencies through cloud-based information exchanges \[[5](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_005), [6](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_006)\]. Moreover, real-time manufacturing is made possible by the use of automation, advanced sensors, social media, and medical applications to forecast demand globally and track medication compliance \[[7](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_007), [8](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_008)\].

AI is capable of recognizing critical relationships in unprocessed documents that can be utilized for the diagnosis, mitigation, and treatment of disease. Almost any discipline of medicine can benefit from the application of several of the more recent techniques employed in the emerging field of computational thinking. To resolve intricate clinical issues, it is necessary to confront the challenge of collecting, assessing, and implementing a plethora of knowledge \[[9](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_009)\]. The development of numerous drug molecules from a chemical space is a difficult task due to the absence of suitable technologies. However, the drug development process can be enhanced by integrating artificial intelligence (AI) \[[10](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_010)\]. The healthcare sector has experienced a substantial increase in the utilization of robotic technologies as a result of AI. Healthcare is undergoing a revolution due to robotics and AI, which are improving patient care, evaluation, and treatment. AI systems can examine sizable datasets, including metabolomic, proteomic, and genomic data, to pinpoint possible therapeutic targets and clarify the workings of diseases \[[11](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_011), [12](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_012)\]. Scientists can learn more about the molecular causes of different ailments and find possible targets for developing drugs through the process. Furthermore, promising drug candidates can be found in large compound libraries using AI-driven virtual screening techniques, which are more effective than conventional high-throughput screening techniques. AI algorithms can forecast the pharmacokinetic, toxicity, and biological activity of possible drug candidates \[[13](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_013), [14](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_014)\]. As a result, less money and time are required for experimental validation. By employing deep learning (DL) techniques to forecast the properties of potential compounds, AI can facilitate the development of novel drug molecules from the ground up. Machine learning (ML) algorithms, known as generative adversarial networks (GANs), are capable of generating novel molecular structures that exhibit the desired biological function and drug-like properties \[[15](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_015), [16](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_016)\].

The effects of drug-drug interactions can be forecasted and mined using AI. To simplify the extraction of drug-drug interactions, researchers integrated kernel-based and feature-based methods with ML methodologies \[[17](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_017)\]. Certain researchers implemented an automated drug-drug interaction detection method to enhance drug safety surveillance in a hospital setting \[[18](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_018)\]. AI can be employed to predict the effects of drug-drug interactions. To extract drug-drug interactions efficiently, researchers integrated kernel-based and feature-based techniques with ML approaches \[[18](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_018)\]. ML can considerably enhance pharmacovigilance by enabling its models to predict a significant number of potential drug-drug interactions by learning from a limited amount of drug-drug interaction combinations \[[19](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_019)\]. Additionally, ML can be employed to identify populations that are susceptible to adverse drug reactions (ADRs) and to direct personalized care. Moreover, ML methodologies can be utilized to pinpoint specific patients, particularly those vulnerable to fluoropyrimidine toxicity. Researchers employed ML methods to analyze toxicity patterns, enabling them to predict the prevalence of individuals with DPD-related toxicity. The model may yet prove advantageous in the future, notwithstanding the risk of considerable overfitting \[[20](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_020)\].

## 4.2 Key technologies in smart pharma

Smart pharma involves a diverse array of stakeholders, such as hospitals, physicians, patients, and research facilities. It is a complex, organic entity that encompasses a variety of functions, including health decision-making, hospital administration, medical research, diagnosis and treatment, and illness prevention and monitoring. Information technologies, including blockchain, IoT, ML, DL, and big data, are the foundations of smart pharma, as shown in [Fig. 4.1](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_fig_001).

![Fig. 4.1: Graphical representation of various key technologies involved in smart pharma.](graphic/converted/b_9783111574288-004_fig_001.png)

Fig. 4.1: Graphical representation of various key technologies involved in smart pharma.

### 4.2.1 Big data in smart pharma

Digitalization of healthcare data has aided in the expansion and advancement of big data. Due to the data’s explosive growth in the last several years, a new field known as “big data” was established. Health care is becoming more mobile and intelligent thanks to big data analysis and other smart healthcare solutions that also reduce costs and increase efficiency. Chemists can use statistical analysis to predict pharmacologic outcomes and recognize the patients who might benefit most from pharmaceutical treatments, which can aid them in better understanding the risks and side effects of pharmaceuticals \[[21](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_021), [22](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_022), [23](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_023)\]. Big data has emerged as a popular research topic in recent years due to its possible uses in health and medicine. It has demonstrated promise in the treatment of conditions like Alzheimer’s disease by utilizing big data-driven systems for clinical decision-making. Electronic medical record optimization has also been achieved through the application of big data techniques. Toxicology, pharmaceuticals, and pharmacology have produced data regarding chemical properties, pharmacokinetics, clinical behaviors, and drug toxicity. High-throughput screening (HTS) and next-generation sequencing (NGS) have led to a significant increase in the amount of data \[[24](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_024), [25](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_025)\].

By reducing the need for in-person visits, enhancing patient outcomes, and guaranteeing that clinicians have immediate access to all patient medical records, big data analysis will enable digital health care. Through digital health and aggregated real-time data, big data analysis, which offers more chances for proactive intervention and a more comprehensive picture of patients’ circumstances, will become a crucial tool for healthcare providers \[[26](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_026), [27](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_027)\]. The present epoch is defined by the incessant proliferation of data streams, attributable to the extensive use of electronic medical records, the vast quantities of data relayed by various sensors, and the information patients disseminate on social media platforms. The healthcare sector consistently produces significant volumes of data, including genetic information, medical records, imaging data, and health-related behaviors. Effective data utilization by healthcare organizations can aid in tracking illnesses, medical decision-making, and public health management \[[28](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_028)\]. To solve the problem, electronic health records, or EHRs, are digital collections of electronic patient health data. The use of EHRs is growing in developing nations. These records can be shared between various healthcare settings via a robust network connection. Since digital health records can be shared, revised, and altered across various healthcare systems, there is less chance of missing any important paperwork, which contributes to the reduction of duplicate data. EHR initiatives provide instant benefits to hospital management, physicians, and patients \[[29](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_029)\].

### 4.2.2 Blockchain in smart pharma

Pharmaceutical development and research is a multi-year, complex process that includes drug discovery, drug development, and regulatory approval. Blockchain is a decentralized computer network that provides its members with access to a secure ledger of transactions and operates without a central server. By timestamping each transaction made within the network, it eliminates the need for an outside organization. Every network node maintains its local ledger and maintains a local copy of the record. Modest changes made to the localized ledger are reflected across the network. Beyond just cryptocurrencies, blockchain technology enables the creation and monitoring of any sort of digital asset. Within the pharmaceutical supply chain, a product created by a manufacturer and registered on the network can be transferred to other participants like cryptocurrency, as it is considered a digital asset. Once again, blockchain ranks first for privacy protection. Each member of a blockchain network is given a key pair that serves as their identity \[[30](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_030)\]. The blockchain approach maintains patient privacy and data security while offering a special benefit for patient recruitment. Patient recruitment times are shortened by using blockchain distributed ledgers because anonymously registered patients can be linked to various clinical trial sites. As a result, the researcher can select the necessary subjects from a group of patients by the study plan. Proposals have also been made for a blockchain structure that involves multiple contracts for clinical trials to manage the studies and involve patients \[[31](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_031)\]. Also, the clinical trials are tracked using the blockchain technique, which encourages others to participate in clinical research while also instilling confidence in the outcomes. Because it can result in fewer clinical trials where positive results are covered selectively, transparency is an essential part of drug discovery research. Consequently, applying blockchain technology to clinical trials encourages improved relationships between sponsors and different clinical research sites in addition to enhancing data integrity and transparency \[[32](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_032)\].

### 4.2.3 ML in smart pharma

The initial phase of conventional drug development is the identification of a target protein that is related to a disease. The process is resource-intensive as well as time-consuming. The procedure entails the examination of extensive chemical libraries in search of compounds that interact with the target. As a result, the drug-like properties are improved through iterative optimization procedures \[[33](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_033)\]. The strategy is typically distinguished by a protracted implementation process and a high rate of attrition. Nonetheless, the paradigm is seeing a transition due to the application of ML techniques. The forecasting of biological activity, dissolution, stability, toxicity, absorption, distribution, metabolism, and elimination (ADMET) characteristics of prospective drug candidates exemplifies an effective application of ML. Using the analysis of extensive datasets that include physicochemical properties, activity profiles, and molecular structures, ML algorithms can detect connections and patterns that humans may have overlooked. Because of this, it is no longer necessary to conduct costly and time-consuming experimental studies to ascertain how a substance will operate in biological structures \[[34](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_034)\]. Compound screening and selection are acknowledged applications of ML in drug discovery. Novel molecules can be anticipated to demonstrate activity against a given target by ML models trained on existing active and inactive compounds \[[35](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_035)\].

### 4.2.4 Deep learning in smart pharma

Across a variety of domains, such as computer gaming, speech recognition, natural language processing, computer vision, and autonomous vehicles, DL has achieved remarkable success. It has reaped the rewards of the endlessly expanding availability of computational capacity and the expansion of the volume of data. DL is distinguished from the majority of other ML methods by the adaptability of its neural network architecture. The designs consist of fully connected feed-forward networks, recurrent neural networks (RNNs), and convolutional neural networks (CNNs). The natural application of multilayered feed-forward neural networks for biological function predictions has resulted from the continued application of single-layer NNs in QSAR modeling, as data size as well as processing power have increased \[[36](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_036)\]. It was not anticipated a few years ago that RNNs would be used in de novo design, which has been a somewhat unexpected development. CNNs have demonstrated amazing success in computer vision since high-throughput imaging technology has become widely available, and they are currently a popular choice for processing biological images. In recent years, DL applications have significantly accelerated pharmaceutical research and drug discovery. Their utility has exceeded expectations and demonstrated promise in addressing various drug discovery problems. Their numerous applications in scientific and technological fields, particularly in biomedicine and bioinformatics, are apparent. The automated synthesis of novel chemical compounds with defined characteristics, the prediction of molecular structure and function, and the sophisticated image analysis skills of DL render it an attractive contender for drug discovery. The application of DL can benefit every stage of the drug discovery process, including target identification, prognostic biomarker discovery, drug development, design, and prediction of synergistic antagonism \[[37](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_037)\].

### 4.2.5 Internet of things in smart pharma

The pharmaceutical industry is one where Internet of Things (IoT) is important. Scholars worldwide are endeavoring to implement this fascinating technology within the pharmaceutical sector. Because of their mutual internet connectivity, most researchers predict how much work they are going to do in the field of the next-generation medicinal product revolution. The network uses dynamic data to connect to things, including appliances, cars, trucks, and industrial equipment, as well as other Internet-connected utilities \[[38](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_038)\]. The IoT is the term used to describe the physical connection of software, embedded devices, and electronic sensors, which enables the sharing of data across a network from any location. IoT’s primary contributions to the pharmaceutical industry are its ability to track health issues, forecast patient-related future events through the use of evolutionary technologies, transform data, save costs, and save lives \[[39](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_039)\]. Radio Frequency Identification (RFID) technology has shown great promise for the pharmaceutical industry’s manufacturing and supply chain processes. But unlike bar codes, Internet of Things (IoT) devices are never scanned because they communicate real-time data to other devices through the Internet. IoT is essential to the management of all services because it provides medical services in the field of pharmaceuticals \[[40](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_040)\]. In addition, IoT could be helpful in monitoring and streamlining various departments for real-time control and monitoring to improve production efficiency during the pharmaceutical product’s manufacturing. IoT can be used in pharmaceutical supply chain management and warehousing to monitor the drug product’s storage conditions in real-time and to improve visibility, which will increase operational effectiveness \[[41](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_041)\].

## 4.3 Innovative training methods in smart pharma

The Indian pharmaceutical industry is undergoing significant transformation, and in the intricate and cutthroat business climate, an organization’s ability to succeed is determined by a wide range of factors. Any company’s capacity to foster a culture of continuous learning is one of the critical elements that can contribute to its success. To obtain a general competitive advantage, the learning must concentrate on all employee ranks. Pharma L&D has additionally demonstrated its capacity for creative problem-solving to guarantee that training is not only an activity but rather fits in with business and functions as a true business partner. Gamification and virtual training are two cutting-edge methods in smart pharma \[[42](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_042)\]. Virtual reality (VR) has been shown in most research conducted over the last ten years to be safe, effective, and to increase user satisfaction to a high degree. With the rising affordability, adaptability, and portability of VR technology, it can now be used for medical treatment in inpatient as well as outpatient settings \[[43](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_043)\]. Studies indicate that virtual reality is an efficacious approach for alleviating pain related to numerous medical and psychological problems \[[44](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_044), [45](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_045)\]. It has been identified as a potential treatment for a diverse array of conditions, such as migraines, pruritus, diabetic neuropathy, cancer, and injuries that result in acute or chronic pain \[[46](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_046), [47](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_047)\]. VR has also demonstrated promise in the treatment of pain following medical or surgical procedures, such as dental work, IV-line implantation, and dressing changes for wounds. VR helps enhance the effectiveness of analgesics and supports conventional pharmacotherapies \[[48](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_048), [49](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_049)\]. Five of the seven studies that were reviewed showed that VR is a useful nonpharmacologic supplement to conventional opioid analgesic therapies \[[50](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_050), [51](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_051), [52](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_052), [53](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_053)\]. In those studies, both adult and pediatric patients undergoing uncomfortable medical procedures reported less pain and anxiety when using VR \[[54](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_054), [55](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_055), [56](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_056)\]. Analgesia given either with or without a virtual reality game was the subject of a different randomized trial. The trial showed that the combination of virtual reality and analgesics proved significantly more efficient than analgesics alone in reducing pain in children receiving burn treatment \[[57](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_057)\]. Additionally, research has been conducted to ascertain whether the advantages of virtual reality persevere beyond the initial therapy regimen \[[58](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_058)\].

Virtual reality offers numerous benefits compared to alternative computer-based methodologies utilized in drug design and discovery, including enhanced three-dimensional visualization of compounds and targets. The FDA’s approval of medications has not matched the rising costs of drug development in recent decades, necessitating a swift and effective approach to drug development. Moreover, numerous unknown pharmacological targets exist, fully immersed in virtual reality using molecular modeling to identify and create innovative drug candidates with significant potential. Visualization techniques are essential for drug design and discovery when examining the interactions between ligands – potential pharmaceuticals – and macromolecules. Precise visualization of ligand binding to extensive complex structures, such as proteins, is essential. Virtual reality (VR) enables scientists to concentrate on ligand-binding sites and other pertinent regions while monitoring the dynamics of protein structural configurations \[[59](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_059), [60](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_060)\].

Gamification involves incorporating game elements or features into a product to enhance user engagement. While it may seem ridiculous to learn topics like physiology or pharmacokinetics by engaging in video games, it has been shown to enhance the confidence and skill sets of healthcare professionals. Conventional lectures concentrate on teaching a specific subject to learners, enabling them to apply the knowledge in a clinical context. There is much more to applying the knowledge that is taught in school than just memorizing it. It requires the application of critical thinking and decision-making abilities, as well as the cultivation of effective communication skills with patients and other healthcare providers. By utilizing class time to practice integrating their knowledge, the concept of gamification offers an additional opportunity to exercise these skills \[[61](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_061)\]. Educational materials incorporate game design components, including game mechanics, as part of the methodology. Gamification is dedicated to providing consumers with motivational affordances, or “games,” to enhance their motivation. Research has revealed that the activities associated with the implementation of gamification exhibit variations \[[62](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_062), [63](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_063), [64](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_064)\]. With the following mode of delivery, learners can be targeted with motivational tools like progress trackers, leaderboards, points, and feedback \[[63](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_063)\]. Studies reveal that depending on the individual user, gamification’s effect on intrinsic motivation varies considerably. Gamification has the potential to both “facilitate and undermine intrinsic drive” by appealing to people’s feelings of autonomy and competence \[[64](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_064)\]. Furthermore, the literature currently in publication is ambiguous regarding the duration over which gamification affects intrinsic motivation. The challenge of comparing the efficacy of the strategy is made challenging by the imprecise description of gamification and the widespread use of various variations in the approach. Numerous studies back up the need to conduct additional comprehensive and repeatable research on the effects of gamification. One benefit of gamification that has been repeatedly demonstrated to be true in literature reports is its positive effect on user satisfaction \[[65](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_065), [66](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_066), [67](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_067)\]. Commercial escape rooms cater to a wide range of people. Enigmas must be modified for educational escape rooms since they are made for an extremely limited target audience, which is typically students studying a particular subject \[[68](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_068)\]. Therefore, a thorough understanding of the target students’ abilities and attitudes is crucial for the Room of Escape to be successful \[[69](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_069)\]. These factors suggest that the installation of an escape room in a school environment should happen after a significant portion of the curriculum has been taught to the students, allowing them to actively participate in solving the challenges \[[70](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_070)\].

## 4.4 Industry-academic collaboration

Academic medical centers are spending money to ensure that their partnerships with pharmaceutical companies are successful because there is renewed interest in these kinds of partnerships. Information should be shared in both directions during these collaborations. Academic colleagues in the field of pharmacology can impart invaluable knowledge to one another. The majority of academic institutions stand to gain from exchanging experiences in the areas of technology, project management, knowledge management, and operational excellence. Pharma must find a way to grant academics access to its vast chemical libraries, private data sets, state-of-the-art instruments, and technological platforms. Strategic, cooperative innovation is the only way to keep up with the rate of biomedical discovery and to fully realize the potential of new targets or disease biomarkers. Pharma’s current business tactics rely on collaborations, acquisitions, and integrations to find clinical candidates. Effective collaborations with academic institutions to tackle noteworthy scientific inquiries and integrate novel targets into treatment approaches are imperative to feed the forthcoming pharmaceutical pipeline with inventive pharmaceuticals \[[71](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_071), [72](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_072)\].

There is a constant search for new translational models, facilitating technologies, and therapeutic mechanisms. Pharmaceutical research used to be frequently divided into different departments within organizations, but this is no longer the case. Similarly, collaboration with the private sector has become more valuable, and academic drug discovery has undergone significant change in recent years. It is worth more than cash. To increase the practical significance of these publicly funded discoveries, these multidisciplinary collaborations bring together the most creative concepts and methods for identifying new therapeutic targets, addressing gaps in translational chemical biology, and developing new chemistry and technologies \[[73](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_073), [74](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_074)\]. Training courses can be added to the curriculum as a way to help students develop their skills. Students can be required to attend courses in their areas of interest throughout their time as graduates. A foundational understanding of work skills will enable freshmen to achieve their objectives. Such a training program can be carried out as a joint venture between industry and institutions and should place greater emphasis on industrially relevant areas. As part of academic collaborations with industry, industries should step forward and align the educational content with their requirements for the development of training modules. Industry-academia interaction ought to be promoted and taken into account as a component of education. Numerous IT companies have begun collaborating with universities and engineering colleges \[[75](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_075)\].

University-industry collaborative research and development (UIC R&D) is a term that encompasses pharmaceutical innovation processes that are more transparent. In the process, scientists and industry-based organizations collaborate to identify technological breakthroughs and knowledge, and subsequently develop new drugs based on these discoveries \[[76](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_076), [77](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_077), [78](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_078)\]. Molecular, biological, and chemical pharmacology knowledge is advanced through formal or informal collaborations between universities and industry groups. The goal of these collaborations is to translate the knowledge into new drug creation and commercialization initiatives that are beneficial to society and the economy. It is known as pharmaceutical UIC R&D \[[79](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_079), [80](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_080)\]. Using diverse UIC research and development strategies, such as establishing technology transfer offices (TTOs) and initiating academic start-up company incubation programs, universities endeavor to enhance and fortify the ties between academia and industry to advance an efficient and productive innovation ecosystem for novel drug discovery \[[81](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_081)\]. However, as academic and industrial organizations have different goals, guiding principles, and schedules, there are still very few drug development processes that are fully and flawlessly driven by university-industry collaboration. These elements play a part in conflict resolution and negotiation issues. There is little, if any, comprehensive research on the composition and coordination of pharmaceutical R&D partnerships between academic institutions and private businesses. Due to the phenomenon, a large number of scholarly contributions have focused on the outcomes of pharmaceutical R&D in one or a small number of university-industry collaborations. Gaining insight into the causes and factors influencing university-industry R&D in pharmaceuticals can help increase productivity and effectiveness \[[82](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_082)\]. The potential for productive specialization in a variety of institutional contexts may be presented by collaborating with others. Rather than incorporating industry practices into academia, industry collaboration could improve the academic framework by decreasing patenting rates and increasing publication rates. Collaboration between academia and industry in research areas with both scientific and commercial potential may result in a more productive division of tasks and responsibilities, enabling each party to concentrate on the aspects of their institutional environments that are most rewarding: for academia, it would be the development of deeper scientific discoveries, while for business, it would be the commercialization of these discoveries. Previous research has suggested that academic institutions have a comparative advantage in the investigation of more fundamental, basic concerns, while companies have a relative advantage in development and commercialization. Depending on their interests, scientists tend to specialize in either industry or academia. Scientists who are more inclined to enter academia are those who have a stronger “taste for science” or who prefer non-financial rewards; on the other hand, scientists who are more inclined toward applied research and financial incentives tend to enter the industry. Furthermore, the intellectual and material exchange that occurs within academic institutions is especially conducive to curiosity-driven research, while the resources and experience of businesses facilitate effective commercialization \[[83](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_083), [84](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_084)\].

## 4.5 Challenges faced by smart pharma

[Figure 4.2](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_fig_002) illustrates the hurdles and downsides that must be considered, notwithstanding the potential benefits of AI in drug discovery. The acquisition of pertinent data is a major challenge. Extensive data aggregations are generally necessary for the training of AI methodologies. The reliability and precision of the results can sometimes be compromised by the limited number, inconsistent quality, or poor quality of the available data \[[85](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_085), [86](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_086), [87](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_087)\]. It offers an important quantity of data, which is used by models that are trained gradually. The company suffered greatly from the data acquisition model via different databases because it was more expensive. Furthermore, a substantial amount of trustworthy data is required to guarantee the pharmaceutical analysis. Another obstacle keeping the pharmaceutical and biological sectors from fully adopting AI is the shortage of skilled personnel to oversee the AI-based platforms \[[88](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_088)\]. The lack of funding for small organizations and the use of AI and ML by large corporations, such as pharmaceutical companies, to replace their human workforce, lead to job losses. In ML processes, decisions are generated by the input data; however, the processes from input to output are not transparent. The input data is transformed through several intricate layers in CNNs, leading to unpredictable behavior in the end \[[89](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_089)\]. Furthermore, the absence of a rationale for predictions that are specific to black-box ML models has an impact on ML predictions as a result of human error in data interpretation. But logical models and decision trees, like relational ML, don’t show the fundamental problem. The dependence on model performance is impacted by human users’ incapacity to understand anticipated outcomes. There are still concerns regarding prediction confidence despite the ML models’ encouraging initial results. When an algorithm attempts to search domains outside of its applicability domain, it is more likely to result in a pathological failure in the hypothesis produced by ML techniques \[[90](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_090)\].

![Fig. 4.2: Various challenges faced by smart pharma.](graphic/converted/b_9783111574288-004_fig_002.png)

Fig. 4.2: Various challenges faced by smart pharma.

The reliability and accuracy of the results can sometimes be compromised by uneven quality, insufficient quantity, or substandard quality of the available data. Another ethical problem is the possibility that AI-based solutions may provoke inquiries over prejudice and equity. An ML system may produce inaccurate or unfair forecasts if the training set contains biased or unrepresentative data. It is imperative to consider the ethical and equitable implementation of AI in the development of novel pharmaceutical products. The knowledge and experience that human researchers have acquired, as well as traditional experimental procedures, cannot be supplanted by current AI-based methods. As AI is limited to making predictions based on available data, human researchers are subsequently required to validate and interpret the results \[[91](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_091), [92](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_092), [93](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_093), [94](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_094), [95](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_095), [96](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_096), [97](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_097)\]. Pharmaceutical companies have access to millions of compounds in their databases for drug development, which could prove too numerous for conventional machine-learning tools to manage. A computational model can employ the quantitative structure-activity relationship (QSAR) to rapidly forecast several chemicals or essential physicochemical characteristics, such as log P or log D. Nonetheless, these models exhibit little proficiency in forecasting intricate biological characteristics, like the effectiveness and side effects of certain drugs. The lack of experimental validations, the data derived from experimental errors in training sets, and the restricted size of the training sets hinder the advancement of QSAR-based models \[[98](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_098), [99](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_099)\].

The problem of counterfeiting is one of the main application areas for blockchain technology, which has been most recently adopted by the pharmaceutical industry. Information security and privacy are two benefits of blockchain technology, which is largely decentralized. More processing power is needed. Roughly half of the power is dedicated to thwarting malicious user attacks; without efficient tracking and tracing, users risk losing goods and becoming victims of robbery \[[100](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_100)\]. Blockchain technology can enable safe, international banking transactions, but interoperability issues present a significant obstacle. Blockchain operations can be standardized through the use of distributed ledger technology (DLT). Additional research is necessary to determine the most effective methods for implementing DLT applications, despite the International Telecommunication Union (ITU)’s efforts to identify and standardize DLT. The deployment of smart IoT applications, cloud services, and periphery computing has significantly improved the availability of data \[[101](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_101)\]. The deployment of blockchain faces numerous challenges as a result of the massive growth, including issues with data management, privacy, redundancy, and accessibility. Furthermore, it has attempted to use blockchain technology to expedite medical transactions, protect patients, and enable tracking and monitoring due to misbehavior and poorly managed supply chains \[[102](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_102), [103](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_103)\]. It’s also critical to guarantee interoperability amongst various healthcare systems and equipment. Strong interoperability solutions are required for effective communication and data exchange among the different hospitals and systems. The blockchain network must be capable of accommodating increased transaction rates without experiencing performance degradation as the volume of healthcare data increases. Careful design and enforcement procedures are required for blockchain deployments to abide by strict data protection laws like GDPR and HIPAA \[[104](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_104), [105](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_105), [106](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_106), [107](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_107), [108](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_108)\].

The researchers claim that it is challenging to integrate blockchain technology with the present healthcare system. Since many hospitals continue to operate with legacy systems, careful planning is necessary to ensure a seamless transition and backward compatibility. In addition, it is critical to address blockchain’s energy consumption problems, particularly those related to proof-of-work consensus. Healthcare organizations should consider blockchain solutions that are environmentally friendly and energy-efficient \[[109](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_109), [110](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_110), [111](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_111), [112](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_112)\]. Managing storage capacity presents another difficulty on the front. Because blockchain was developed to store and analyze transactional data, which is restricted in scope, it does not necessitate a significant amount of storage. Ethical concerns also pose a challenge, as AI-based solutions may raise concerns regarding impartiality and bias \[[113](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_113)\]. For example, the forecasts generated by ML system may be erroneous or inequitable if the training data is biased or unrepresentative \[[114](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_114)\]. Considering the ethical and fair implementation of AI in the creation of new medicinal molecules is crucial \[[115](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_115)\]. Various strategies and techniques can be utilized to overcome the challenges that AI faces in the domain of chemical medicine. One approach is to employ data augmentation, which involves generating synthetic data to enhance current datasets. The accuracy and consistency of outcomes can be enhanced by increasing the quantity and diversity of data utilized to train ML algorithms \[[116](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_116)\]. An alternative method that seeks to deliver succinct and intelligible elucidations for the predictions produced by ML algorithms is the application of explainable AI (XAI) approaches \[[117](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_117)\]. It has the potential to reduce concerns regarding the impartiality and bias of AI-based techniques \[[113](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_113)\] and to provide a more thorough understanding of the fundamental assumptions and mechanisms that underlie the predictions.

DL approaches are not interpretable, in addition to the constraints on data. When it comes to DL methods, they are viewed as a mystery in contrast to traditional ML methods, which frequently undergo a thorough validation analysis based on mathematical reasoning. Even though DL outperforms ML on the majority of tasks, researchers frequently are unable to comprehend why ML produces such excellent results. If a DL model produces a novel outcome that deviates from prior research, its interpretability issues render the result unsatisfactory. Unlike other fields, drug discovery is rich in knowledge about the regulatory systems of biological pathways, the metabolic processes of molecules, and the mechanisms of action of compounds. A comprehensive understanding of pertinent biological processes is crucial to guarantee the safety and efficacy of medications. These processes include the physical features of medications, the proteins they bind to in the body, the binding mechanisms, the biological reactions they elicit, and their metabolic pathways. DL is restricted to the acceptance of input and the generation of predicted output; it is incapable of providing a comprehensive explanation of the process by which the output is generated \[[118](#9783111573861_004895.xhtml#b_9783111574288-004_ref_118)\].

## 4.6 Conclusion

The pharmaceutical industry is experiencing rapid growth due to digitalization, which uses information shared via system integration, mobile phones, and other technologies to understand consumer demand and improve supply chain efficiency. The digital revolution in healthcare is bringing about a “connected health” movement, improving patient outcomes, reducing per capita costs, and enhancing the care experience. Robotic technologies improve patient care, diagnosis, and treatment, while AI can find significant relationships in unprocessed documents and aid in drug development. Drug-drug interactions can be retrieved, and their effects can be predicted by machine learning algorithms. By eliminating the need for in-person visits and guaranteeing that clinicians have immediate access to all medical records, big data analysis is enabling digital health care. The pharmaceutical industry is using blockchain technology to expedite regulatory approval, drug development, and discovery. The IoT is also being used to track health issues, forecast patient-related events, transform data, save costs, and save lives. The Indian pharmaceutical industry is undergoing significant transformation, and fostering a culture of continuous learning is crucial for success. Two cutting-edge methods in smart pharma include gamification and virtual training. Educational escape rooms in higher education should focus on student learning and industry-academic collaboration to advance knowledge related to molecular, biological, and chemical pharmacology.
