# Chapter 5 Drug manufacturing and quality control with artificial intelligence

#### Abstract

Artificial intelligence (AI) uses advanced instruments and networks to mimic human intelligence. Artificial intelligence is increasingly used in the pharmaceutical industry to automate and manage large data sets. Quality by design (QbD) and AI can improve product quality, reduce waste, and speed up production in the pharmaceutical industry. The chapter provides information on drug manufacturing and quality control using AI in the pharma industry. The medical research sector must integrate technologies like blockchain and AI into its core competencies to thrive. The objective of the European Union’s AI-based manufacturing compliance regulations is examined, aiming to align technology with the EU’s core values, rights, and principles. The body of research on the Internet of Things privacy and security measures was reviewed, along with the myths surrounding widely used technologies that make extreme security promises. To raise the bar for pharmaceutical handling of data, sharing, processing, assessment, and classification based on outcomes, the chapter discusses cutting-edge blockchain and AI methods.

Keywords:  drug,  production,  quality,  management,  security,  regulatory compliance,  quality-by-design,  

## 5.1 Introduction

The application of artificial intelligence (AI) is growing across many domains in society, chief among them being the pharmaceutical industry. As processes become more digital, it’s harder to acquire, evaluate, as well as apply knowledge to complicated clinical problems \[[1](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_001)\]. It encourages AI use because it can automatically handle large amounts of data. AI simulates human intelligence using advanced tools and networks \[[2](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_002), [3](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_003)\]. However, it doesn’t threaten to completely replace people in the physical world. AI makes use of hardware and software to perform independent analysis, learning, and decision-making to accomplish predefined objectives \[[4](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_004)\]. After training, the ability to learn over time is frequently lost when an AI system is used to control procedures like clinical trials or manufacturing. Since the recent adoption of quality by design (QbD) techniques, the pharmaceutical industry has grown, and Industry 4.0 initiatives indicate rapid development \[[5](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_005)\]. Successful treatment requires choosing the right target for drug molecule synthesis. Numerous proteins, a few of which are overexpressed, are involved in the disease’s progression. Therefore, predicting the desired protein’s molecular structure is crucial to designing a drug molecule specifically targeting that disease. AI can facilitate drug discovery through structural analysis by modeling the three-dimensional configuration of proteins that corresponds to the chemical milieu of the target protein site. It aids in forecasting a compound’s target impact and ensuring safety before synthesis or manufacture \[[6](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_006)\].

AI has the potential to supplant trial-and-error methodologies. QSPR enables many computational techniques to address formulation design challenges, including stability, dissolution, porosity, and more \[[7](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_007), [8](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_008)\]. A feedback mechanism monitors the entire process and makes occasional adjustments to power decision-support tools. Based on the medication’s physicochemical properties, they choose the excipient type, amount, and type using rule-based systems \[[9](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_009)\]. AI offers a plethora of options for streamlining development and production manufacturing processes. AI can result in improvements in quality. Additionally, by using AI in several ways, including accelerating processes, it can reduce material waste and increase production efficiency \[[10](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_010)\]. To ensure quality and produce the pharmaceutical composition within the intended timeframe, a well-planned, scientific approach utilizing AI is required for quality assurance and control. Researchers examine the characteristics of pharmacological substances as well as how they interact with equipment, unit activities, and other substances. Applications include correlations, the first-principle model, heuristics, and decision trees. Among other things, decisions about machine sizes, excipients, and manufacturing processes are made using the information. To increase product quality and reduce waste, AI is used in conjunction with related networks, methods, and tools. Pharmaceutical companies will benefit as a result. To guarantee product quality, quality by design, or QbD, is applied as shown in [Fig. 5.1](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_fig_001).

![Fig. 5.1: Graphical representation of the utilization of AI and laboratories in the pharmaceutical industry.](graphic/converted/b_9783111574288-005_fig_001.png)

Fig. 5.1: Graphical representation of the utilization of AI and laboratories in the pharmaceutical industry.

The complexity of manufacturing has grown compared to previous years due to notable advancements and recent advancements in pharmaceutical technology. The quality and efficacy of a tested product are also essential for the intricate process. Modern pharmaceutical manufacturing systems incorporate human expertise into machines to improve processes. AI in pharmaceutical production may boost the industry \[[11](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_011)\]. AI tools will help mechanize many pharmaceutical activities. Not only can it help with manufacturing process issues, but it can also support numerical simulation. By utilizing other state-of-the-art manufacturing technologies, AI can be incorporated into the medication manufacturing process to achieve the desired level of outcomes. Potential applications of AI in pharma production processes were listed in a discussion paper released by the U.S. FDA. AI-based pharmaceutical quality control and assurance systems are anticipated to yield benefits \[[12](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_012)\]. Pharmaceutical companies use bands to ensure product quality, even though they may be inaccurate. AI applications in the pharmaceutical sector can forecast minute changes that may affect the efficacy and security of the products. AI-based pharmaceutical quality assurance and control can enhance process accuracy and product quality \[[13](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_013)\]. Moreover, preclinical lead molecule failure rates can be decreased by using computational methods with multi-objective refinement. AI in drug design uses computer software to evaluate, explore, and learn from enormous quantities of pharmaceutical data to find new therapeutic compounds. Combining ML advances in a unified, completely automated way achieves it \[[14](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_014)\]. Machine learning (ML)-based methodologies, a derivative of AI, emphasizes the transformation of extensive biomedical large data into novel insights and practical knowledge rather than the theoretical advancement of intricate physicochemical principles \[[15](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_015)\]. These days, deep learning (DL) paradigms and other AI technologies have incredible feature extraction and generalization capabilities that make them highly intriguing for drug design. While DL approaches can automatically extract characteristics from the input data and reorganize fundamental traits into complex characteristics via multi-layer feature extraction, conventional ML approaches use manually generated attributes \[[16](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_016), [17](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_017)\].

## 5.2 AI application in pharmaceutical manufacturing

The pharmaceutical sector is urged to innovate via process analytical technology (PAT) and QbD methodologies. The QbD approach underscores the importance of understanding the product and process in determining the key material attributes (KMAs) and critical process parameters (CPPs) that substantially affect the critical quality attributes (CQAs) of both the product and process. It results in the creation of a design area with acceptable quality standards. Operating inside a space that has been approved by regulations for design does not constitute a change; therefore, production flexibility can be increased. AI primarily refers to computational techniques that carry out tasks that are commonly associated with cognitive processes similar to those of humans, like pattern recognition as well as decision-making. These tasks are carried out by ML techniques in AI, which learn from a dataset and generate a response without explicit programming. As the training dataset expands, ML can adapt the model behavior to continually enhance its performance, which is particularly useful for data-driven manufacturing applications \[[18](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_018), [19](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_019), [20](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_020), [21](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_021), [22](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_022)\]. Additionally, the medical regulatory bodies are becoming more receptive to AI/ML techniques. However, more investigation is needed to prepare for smart manufacturing concerning the ML process’s real-time applicability in the pharmaceutical manufacturing process. ML can use past process data to estimate the crystallization result. For example, researchers used data from various industrial batch crystallizations to create ANN and GA models. Nine descriptors were used to model the crystal density, which, after optimization, led to a notable improvement in the final product and assisted in identifying the critical parameters. Based on a few experimental runs as well as four input variables, the design area of a co-crystallization process could be investigated in a different study. ANN inputs for the operating variables (moisture, supersaturation, agitation speed, along with seeding properties) can also yield a more accurate crystal growth rate estimate than numerous non-linear regressions \[[23](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_023), [24](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_024)\]. Furthermore, the behavior of the compacted powdered mixture within the tablet press must be considered initially when constructing a tableting process. For each filling, the blend’s flowability must be sufficiently high to ensure that almost identical quantities of powder enter the die. Certain researchers employed a feed-forward and back propagation artificial neural network (FF-BP ANN) to forecast the rate at which specific powders would traverse a circular aperture. The input parameters for powders typically include bulk density, tapped density, particle size, aspect ratio, roundness, convexity, and true density. The flow equation proposed by other researchers was less precise than the flow rate values obtained as shown in [Fig. 5.2](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_fig_002) \[[25](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_025), [26](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_026)\].

![Fig. 5.2: Various applications of AI in the manufacturing of pharmaceuticals.](graphic/converted/b_9783111574288-005_fig_002.png)

Fig. 5.2: Various applications of AI in the manufacturing of pharmaceuticals.

With downtime and financial losses becoming more and more important, predictive maintenance, or PdM, has become a popular anomaly prediction technique in the manufacturing system. PdM can forecast the health state using ML algorithms and continuously monitor manufacturing machinery with smart electrical sensors. Preventive, predictive, and run-to-failure (R2F) are the three categories into which maintenance strategies employed by manufacturing companies can be divided. The simplest is R2F since it is only used in the event of a system failure. The direct costs of manufacturing processes will increase, nevertheless, if production lines are stopped, industrial equipment is repaired, and production facilities are adjusted. In contrast, PdM works better when implemented before a system fails because it examines, observes, and prevents early faults. The idea of total productive maintenance (TPM) has been established based on the productivity principle to increase employee satisfaction at all organizational levels, decrease manufacturing costs, and improve the overall performance of production equipment \[[27](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_027)\]. Using ML algorithms to identify when preservation chores are required can save operational expenses. Second, by utilizing statistical techniques, engineering approaches, and historical data, it can produce more accurate predictions. Thirdly, because it can detect faults early on, it can extend the life of industrial equipment and enhance working conditions \[[28](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_028)\]. There are two challenges even though PdM is the most efficient for fault detection. On the one hand, PdM uses ML algorithms, but the quality of the data representation frequently limits the performance. Specifically, poor ML algorithm performance is produced by data that have redundant attributes and low attribute importance \[[29](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_029)\]. Supply chain design and configuration strategic decisions can also be informed by ML techniques. A Bayesian Network-based risk propagation model has been proposed by researchers. The suggested model considers the peculiarities of a supply-chain network structure in addition to the interdependency of different risks to produce specific risk measures. Lastly, a simulation study shows how output risk measures can be used in a supply chain environment \[[30](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_030)\]. By automating risky tasks, they were able to decrease inventory costs through improved supply and demand planning, shorten development cycles, increase engineering productivity, decrease errors, improve safety, increase revenue through improved sales lead identification and cost optimization, and more. As a result, they experienced rapid growth. The novel idea of “intelligent manufacturing” is a clever method of production in which humans and machines collaborate side by side under minimal supervision. Intelligent manufacturing is best demonstrated by the Siemens manufacturing sector. The worker creates programmable logic circuits and uses a virtual factory that mimics a real factory floor to supervise and manage their production. Barcodes let items talk to the machinery that makes them, and the machinery may talk to each other to exchange parts and troubleshoot problems \[[31](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_031), [32](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_032), [33](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_033)\].

Manufacturers are optimizing important indicators of performance and reviewing them in real-time with the help of AI. For instance, customizing a model with virtual programs can improve energy consumption and better anticipate, detect, and prevent staffing and material bottlenecks. More significantly, it notifies the engineers in advance of issues and suggests fixes. AI-enabled manufacturing speeds time to market, reduces waste, cuts costs, and efficiently executes assembly line procedures. It has been demonstrated that utilizing collaborative robots, self-driving cars, and machine-learning algorithms can lower inventory levels and improve warehouse expenses \[[34](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_034)\]. DL is a subclass of ML that utilizes extensive experimental data to learn through advanced approaches such as artificial neural networks. Neurons in multi-layered neural networks generally alter data non-linearly in DL models. Recently, convolutional and recurrent neural networks have been effectively employed in pharmaceutical sciences for various applications in developing accurate dosage formulations. These applications include the detection of tablet defects, prediction of particle flowability, prediction of storage stability, and prediction of drug dissolution profiles \[[35](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_035), [36](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_036), [37](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_037), [38](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_038), [39](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_039)\]. The predictive accuracy of the models must be assessed once the ML modeling process is finished. Researchers looked into how deep neural networks could be used to forecast how long tablets would dissolve. In the following work, the improved maximum dissimilarity algorithm (MD-FIS) was used to separate training and testing subsets of data on various drug formulations that had been gathered through literature mining. Notably, validation and testing of data sets are made possible by MD-FIS, an advanced data selection technique \[[40](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_040)\].

## 5.3 Application of AI in quality control of pharmaceuticals

Based on the “quality philosophy,” which places a high priority on management by objectives, full engagement, sharing of technology, solving issues, adaptability to change, heritage preservation, and people-oriented practices; additionally, the “core values,” which emphasize the significance of system integration as a means, sustainable development for a healthy environment, and pragmatic benefits as an inducement, are the foundation of the organization \[[41](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_041)\]. Participants will need to think through the matter thoroughly and work very hard during the brewing process. Since the term “Industry 4.0” has become highly contested in the industry, the writers have been able to concentrate solely on the development of people in the high-quality expert field, and they have not developed any countermeasures \[[42](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_042)\]. Any AI-based application used in a clinical environment needs to have a quality assurance workflow in place to regularly and individually verify the effectiveness of the AI output.

All validation procedures that confirm the outcomes of AI-based applications created specifically for each patient or machine are included in case-specific quality assurance (QA). The output can be used in subsequent stages of the RT workflow once the QA results are deemed satisfactory. If the quality check’s case-specific assurance is unsuccessful, the output will go through verification. The effectiveness of certain applications may be assessed using a separate secondary algorithm \[[43](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_043)\]. Additionally, recent research has been done on auto-segmentation quality assurance using ML. Using distinct features for every ROI, decision trees can be created to classify the morphology of a particular auto-segmentation input \[[44](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_044)\]. Research and development in the fields of DL and ML have significantly increased in recent years. Researchers have developed increasingly complex algorithms and models owing to the enhanced availability of data and computational capacity, which has markedly progressed in various areas. One area of research that has advanced significantly is natural language processing (NLP) \[[45](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_045), [46](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_046), [47](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_047)\]. Researchers have used DL models such as transformers and recurrent neural networks (RNNs) to construct more accurate and efficient language models. Improved human-machine connection, simpler process automation for customer service, and more sophisticated search engines and automated assistants could all result from these models. Another area that has made significant strides is computer vision \[[48](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_048), [49](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_049), [50](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_050), [51](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_051)\].

More accurate image classification and object recognition have been made possible by convolutional neural networks (CNNs). These models have the potential to improve autonomous driving, manufacturing quality control, and medical imaging analysis \[[52](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_052), [53](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_053), [54](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_054), [55](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_055), [56](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_056), [57](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_057), [58](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_058)\]. DL approaches may autonomously identify and generate abstract features, while conventional ML methods generally necessitate domain expertise for feature construction or engineering \[[59](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_059)\]. In a variety of inspection applications, DL techniques that utilize recurrent neural networks (RNNs), autoencoders, and CNNs demonstrate exceptional outcomes \[[60](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_060)\]. Researchers use a deep ensemble learning model to examine surface flaws on automobile bodies. When the identical task is finished, their method performs better than human inspectors \[[61](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_061)\]. A CNN is used by researchers in \[[62](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_062)\] to locate flaws in textured surfaces. The results demonstrate high defect detection accuracy on a multi-class dataset. Researchers \[[63](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_063)\] identify manufacturing defects in semiconductors using a CNN-based transfer learning technique; however, other researchers \[[64](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_064)\] suggest using a CNN model that can handle time series data. Researchers \[[65](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_065)\] recommend using deep CNNs for sewer system inspection. They claim that CNN-based techniques perform better than manual feature extraction methods using highly variable image data. An optimized VGG model is used by researchers \[[66](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_066)\] for laser welding inspection. A transfer learning methodology is utilized when a diverse array of images is employed to pre-train the VGG model. Researchers \[[67](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_067)\] describe a system for defect identification that integrates Random Forest (RF) and Support Vector Machines (SVM) with a pre-trained AlexNet for feature extraction. The smart quality inspection (SQI) method seeks to improve the model’s efficacy and address many elements affecting the visual inspection process. Outsourcing segments of the inspection process enables the regulation of various task aspects, external influences, and other variables. Research gaps are found through the literature review process, which is closely linked to the development of the assessment algorithm. The strategy for creating SQI proposes a methodology for utilizing AI-driven visual inspection on-site. The inspection task was allocated to CNN, an AI model selected for its unique architecture. The application enables users to upload images of the product as it exits the assembly line. The CNN model will subsequently analyze the images to ascertain whether the product is adequate or defective and whether it has passed or failed the required inspection. Furthermore, the operator has the option to add the results to the inspection record by utilizing the SQI application \[[68](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_068), [69](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_069), [70](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_070)\].

## 5.4 Data management and security

Interoperability is essential for enabling intelligent healthcare data management systems to receive requisite data input, as various data sources often store and process information in many forms. The challenge of obtaining relevant information is apparent in the immense volume of data that is continuously produced. The Office of the National Coordinator for Health Information Technology in the United States recently issued a draft rule regarding information blocking and interoperability, emphasizing the need to grant patients unfettered access to their electronic health records \[[71](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_071), [72](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_072)\]. Securing privacy and interoperability at the same time seems incompatible, which makes it difficult to achieve. Recently, the potential uses of cutting-edge blockchain technology for the administration of medical data have generated a lot of conversation in both academia and business \[[73](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_073), [74](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_074), [75](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_075), [76](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_076)\]. Blockchain technology can enhance and expedite the sharing of private health information by providing users with complete control over their data and privacy, eliminating the necessity for a centralized authority. Data sharing is essential in the realm of chronic diseases due to patient mobility, the involvement of multiple hospitals for diagnosis and treatment owing to specialized centers, the requirement for a “second opinion,” and the use of multiple medications by patients \[[73](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_073), [77](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_077), [78](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_078)\]. Optimizing the pharmaceutical supply chain with blockchain technology can benefit clinical trials and medical research overall \[[79](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_079), [80](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_080), [81](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_081)\].

By storing only the metadata, which may include patient consent, on the blockchain instead of medical records, blockchain technology can guarantee the traceability and inviolability of patient health data. Individual network nodes can store vast and sensitive healthcare data, and their intelligent representation will be kept off-chain \[[73](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_073)\]. An alternate method involves utilizing compliant cloud-based services for temporary storage (i.e., within a timeframe specified by the patient) and data sharing \[[78](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_078)\]. The FHIR chain is a blockchain-based approach for data sharing that encapsulates clinical data by the Fast Healthcare Interoperability Resources (FHIR) standard. The sharing of primary care data will be expedited and simplified while safeguarding privacy through the effective management of on-chain consent and the establishment of access-control mechanisms as specified in the consent. Applications utilizing blockchain technology throughout the supply chain are starting to materialize. These include improving the public health supply chain, protecting medical equipment, improving the usability of healthcare Internet of Things (IoT) devices, and using the immutability and transparency of the blockchain to combat counterfeit medicines \[[79](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_079)\]. Additionally, these applications ensure that the pharmaceutical company retains control over returned drugs. Researchers show how blockchain technology can offer features that improve pharmaceutical traceability in particular and supply chain management in general \[[80](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_080)\]. Ensuring compliance in the pharmaceutical supply chain is crucial, as is confirming the conditions of transportation and storage. Pharmaceutical companies are also moving closer to their objective of obtaining patient record data directly from the source and putting it into an electronic system so that it can be sent gradually and steadily to sponsors or customers. The switch from paper-based data collection to an electronic system is referred to as “electronic data capture.” Unprocessed patient or volunteer data from clinical trials is kept in a software system called an electronic data system, or EDC \[[81](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_081), [82](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_082), [83](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_083), [84](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_084)\]. Before being entered into the system, the information is first written down and then saved as an electronic case report form (eCRF). Clinical trial software is beneficial to all kinds of organizations, including pharmaceutical, biotech, medical, and research. These programs assist with all facets of conducting a clinical trial, encompassing study submission, archiving, and recruitment. These programs are relevant for both small-scale preliminary research and extensive investigations involving thousands of participants. The phrase “clinical data management systems” or “software” refers to the collection of applications available for data management. These programs are essential because they can manage massive volumes of data in multicentric systems or trials. Many software programs, including ORACLE CLINICAL, MACRO, RAVE, CLINTRIAL, eClinicalSuite, EZentry, and others, are utilized as CDM software in pharmacies \[[85](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_085), [86](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_086), [87](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_087)\]. The functionality of these programs is essentially similar, so they cannot outperform one another. These software programs have the drawback of being costly, and for an organization to operate, sophisticated information technology communications must be acquired. Several benefits are provided by the medical records management software, including the ability to expedite study timelines and control costs, provide precise clinical trial predictions, enable robust program modeling, ease recruiting, scheduling, and screening processes, support physicians, and effortlessly adjust for foreign exchange rates \[[88](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_088), [89](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_089), [90](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_090), [91](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_091)\].

The secret to success is ingraining readily available technologies like AI, blockchain, and other technologies into a company’s core competencies \[[92](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_092)\]. To enhance clinical research and attain patient-centricity, the industry must employ technology to create user- and customer-centric interfaces, make decisions based on data for innovative data processing methods, and enhance outcomes \[[93](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_093), [94](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_094)\]. AI could help, for instance, with patient identification and prioritization for medication development and monitoring, which is necessary for standardized drug production and expedited schedules \[[92](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_092)\]. AI and numerical drug design techniques were used to analyze clinical trial data to determine dosage, evaluate the efficacy of medication formulations, and repurpose pharmaceuticals that were already on the market. By incorporating secure and automated data analysis, blockchain facilitates the creation and maintenance of ledgers, or blocks of content, in systems design. Secure recording and analysis of all health-related data allow for prompt updates to be provided to payers, medical professionals, and doctors. Nevertheless, the blockchain would be exceedingly inefficient for storing a substantial volume of records, such as comprehensive electronic medical records or genetic data records, due to the substantial computational resources that would be necessary. It is a substantial defect in blockchain technology, as it complicates the process of querying data within a blockchain \[[95, 96](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_095)\].

However, there are ways to work around the issue with the blockchain and AI algorithms integrated. Risk assessment must be the initial phase in protecting sensitive personal information and PHI within the life sciences and healthcare sectors. Implementing the Cyber Security Framework (CSF) from the National Institute of Standards and Technology (NIST) is advised for establishing a cybersecurity program. The NIST Cyber Security Framework encompasses standards, recommendations, and best practices for managing cyber security in critical infrastructure. But cloud- and AI-based products, services, and technologies are employed to construct business processes utilizing frameworks to develop security initiatives that will enable those employed in the sector of the economy to be ready to defend the sensitive data and able to respond promptly to any incidents or security threats \[[97](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_097), [98](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_098), [99](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_099)\]. To create a comprehensive security program from process and policy viewpoints, researchers began with the NIST Cyber Security Framework (NIST CSF). They then used technology from SAP Cloud, specifically SAP S4/HANA and its AI-based cloud services, to make up for it. A source of veracity and a record system would be the SAP S4/HANA. Digitization and digital transformation of the life science industries are more important than ever. SAP S4/Cloud offerings provide all required products and services along with control features like private cloud services, enabling life science companies to access state-of-the-art technologies and solutions that leverage cloud and AI services. They are still in charge of their private information, and it helps them comply with global privacy and data laws. With the public, private, and hybrid models that SAP S4/HANA cloud offering offers, users can select the one that best suits their needs and yields the highest return on investment (ROI) while maintaining all the controls required to protect their company’s objectives, safeguard sensitive data, and carry on innovating and producing new medications through clinical research and R&D \[[100](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_100), [101](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_101), [102](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_102)\]. Blockchain technology and AI powered by the IoT will improve biomedical and healthcare data ledgers, accelerating biological and clinical research. These assessments are predicated on fundamental aspects of blockchain and AI, including improved security, resilience, decentralized administration, unchangeable audit trails, and above all the reinstatement of characters’ rights. Innovative and state-of-the-art techniques that will improve medical data handling, collaboration, processing, assessment, and result-based classification are being developed using blockchain and AI. The healthcare industry, which is driven by the IoT, is being redefined by blockchain technology. It is achieved through enhanced productivity, access control, advances in technology, safeguarding privacy, and operational data process safety \[[103](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_103)\].

IoT solutions’ security and privacy are rapidly approaching but largely ignored. The research on privacy and security measures in the IoT is reviewed by the study’s authors, who also look at common misconceptions about technologies that claim to be incredibly secure. Researchers discuss user authentication methods and safe key management for fog computing-based IoT environments. The SAKA-FC security system comprises a dual-component framework featuring three-factor key management and a user authentication mechanism \[[104](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_104)\]. In another study, researchers \[[105](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_105)\] examined the safety of implanted medical devices (IMDs), including pacemakers and insulin pumps. The study’s authors propose a three-factor lightweight remote user authorization approach for implantable medical devices, utilizing elliptic curve cryptography. The security scheme illustrates how the recommended scheme should be implemented using the Dolev-Yao threat structure. It is a system model that is linked to the user (U), the Controller Node (CN), and the IMD. The security of remote monitoring is improved by employing a three-factor verification strategy. To guarantee secure communication, it necessitates the establishment of bilateral keys between CN and IMD, as well as mutual authentication between U and CN through the use of a session key. Other researchers have suggested a lightweight data-sharing plan with traceability, or LiST, for mobile health solutions. The system in the model is comprised of healthcare professionals (the data user), the public cloud, WBSN (the data owner), and the Key Generation Center (KGC), which is considered a completely dependable organization that generates public characteristics for the entire system and provides secret keys to data users. The authors assert that LiST provides lightweight user revocation, lightweight test algorithms, lightweight encryption, lightweight backdoor generation using keywords, and lightweight traitor tracing \[[106](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_106)\]. The scheme implements a lightweight revocation mechanism to eradicate unauthorized users and their decryption privileges to safeguard the privacy of the EHR.

## 5.5 Regulatory compliance in AI-based manufacturing

Today’s legislators and regulators have considerably higher standards for corporate governance and compliance. There is a noticeable shift in favor of obligatory and complete corporate trust and transparency. Legislators and regulators in the European Union are considering creating compliance regarding the use of digital technologies, specifically AI, for the first time. A few years ago, the EU Commission released an AI regulation to guarantee that technology is applied in a way that upholds EU ideals, principles, and fundamental rights \[[107](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_107), [108](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_108)\]. An illustration of a measure implemented to ensure the security, transparency, ethics, neutrality, and human oversight of AI is the EU Commission’s AI Act. The EU now categorizes AI applications according to their risk levels, and corresponding norms and regulations have been established based on the classification. For example, social scoring is too dangerous to use, and transparent rules are all that low-risk chatbots need to abide by. Despite the significance of compliance in the framework of digital technologies, neither legal nor digital technology experts are currently providing thorough scientific approaches or focused attention to the issue. Consequently, AI continues to be the sole notable instance in which the European legislature has looked into these matters. The EU Medical Device Regulation imposes stringent regulatory requirements before medical devices, including AI software devices, may be used in clinical practice. The EU MDR \[[109](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_109)\], which replaced the EU Medical Device Directive, must be fulfilled. For the first time, many of the terms and specifications of the EU MDR also apply to medical device software created by healthcare organizations for internal use.

Software meant to offer data for any of the objectives quantified in the explanation of a medical device \[[109](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_109)\], including monitoring, treatment, prevention, diagnosis, and alleviation of disease, is subject to EU MDR regulation. It covers both software that offers a diagnosis or treatment on its own and software that only offers data to help a medical practitioner arrive at the best diagnosis or course of treatment. The EU MDR remains applicable regardless of whether the software is an executable program, dynamic website, web service, script, or even a simple spreadsheet macro. Additionally, the software is considered a medical device if it is intended for medical purposes and is intended to be used with humans or their data, regardless of the complexity of the processing, the risk it poses to the patient or user, whether or not a healthcare professional uses it, or the computing platform on which it is running \[[110](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_110), [111](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_111), [112](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_112)\]. AI could potentially make predictions regarding the complex relationships between drug efficacy and immunological responses to substantially improve drug discovery \[[113](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_113), [114](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_114)\]. AI could also be beneficial for more intricate tasks, such as the analysis of multiple genomic issues, or for simpler ones, such as the best practices for electronic documentation \[[115](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_115), [116](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_116)\]. AI has been deployed to enhance medical reporting related to oncology and radiology \[[117](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_117), [118](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_118)\]. For analytical labs, where protocols and procedures can occasionally total hundreds or even thousands of pages, and where the quantity of pages grows with every new treatment, it is encouraging. AI can quickly create useful methods and predict text based on input data \[[119](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_119)\]. AI language translation tools can also be applied to worldwide shared methods to improve interlaboratory reproducibility. Furthermore, data produced during stability and new batch cGMP testing needs to be carefully examined, and any inconsistencies need to be looked into through quality investigations. AI tools are capable of conducting investigations and proofreading analytical data to find errors \[[120](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_120)\].

In delicate areas, AI systems should be employed with extreme caution, as judgments made using skewed data or incorrect criteria may have far-reaching effects \[[121](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_121)\]. Consequently, these systems necessitate extensive validation that effectively addresses issues that are distinctive to AI, such as the black box problem. Numerous writers speculate that XAI might be useful in the validation, but particular strategies have not yet been created and assessed. Four phases make up the validation concept, which can be carried out repeatedly or step-by-step. The typical design tasks involved in creating and implementing a new IS are handled in the first phase, known as the setup phase. The technical setup and performance phase that follows covers the steps specifically related to AI development to create a dependable AI system. Thus far, the process bears similarities to validation methodologies or black-box AI system performance assessments \[[122](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_122)\]. Predefined performance measures may suffice for AI systems operating in insensitive environments; however, further effort is necessary to verify that the AI system has been developed and is functioning as intended under the proposed validation concept. The third phase of the XAI Assessment offers the recommended XAI approach, which gives an example of how to use decision rules and/or spaces appropriately. The final stage, the stress test, is designed to ascertain the limits of the AI system’s use, building upon the evaluation. A validation approach was developed in a research and development project with the assistance of specialists in technology, academia, industry, and GMP auditing. This strategy was applied with success in the pharmaceutical manufacturing sector \[[123](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_123)\]. The business putting the AI systems into place must still evaluate a few elements of the created validation strategy, though. It was therefore recommended that future research focus on creating and establishing guidelines and standards for sensitive use cases, such as the pharmaceutical industry. It might result in less validation work being needed, which would then make comprehensive validation techniques more feasible for use cases involving less sensitive data \[[124](#9783111573861_006599.xhtml#b_9783111574288-005_ref_124)\].

## 5.6 Conclusion

The pharmaceutical industry is using artificial intelligence more and more to manage massive data volumes and enhance automation. Artificial intelligence can simulate human intelligence through sophisticated tools and networks, but it cannot take the place of humans in the real world. The pharmaceutical industry has been enhanced by quality by design (QbD) techniques, and AI has the potential to enhance product quality, reduce waste, and expedite manufacturing processes. AI can also be used in drug design to assess, learn from, and uncover big data related to pharmaceuticals, integrating machine learning (ML) advancements in a highly automated and unified manner. Intelligent manufacturing, where humans and machines work together under minimal supervision, has been used to optimize performance indicators, improve energy consumption, and predict staffing and material bottlenecks. AI-based applications in clinical settings must have quality assurance tools to confirm the efficacy of the AI output. Scientists have been able to develop object recognition and classification models for autonomous driving, manufacturing quality control, and medical imaging analysis using convolutional neural networks (CNNs). Recurrent neural networks (RNNs), autoencoders, and CNN-based deep learning techniques have produced outstanding results in a variety of inspection applications. Blockchain, artificial intelligence (AI), and other technologies must be incorporated into core competencies for the medical research industry to succeed. The European Union is considering implementing compliance regulations for AI-based manufacturing, aiming to align technology with EU principles, values, and fundamental rights.
