# Chapter 8 AI-based pharmacovigilance and drug safety

#### Abstract

Pharmacovigilance (PV), a scientific and applied field, aims to identify, evaluate, understand, and minimize adverse drug-related effects. The chapter provides information about artificial intelligence-based tools for pharmacovigilance, drug safety, and its regulatory aspects. It encompasses the scientific and applied endeavors made to identify, assess, comprehend, and mitigate the adverse consequences of drugs or possible drug-related issues. ADR detection and reporting, technological AE coding, safety individual reporting, severity assessment, and connection to suspected drugs are among the most significant duties in the PV sector. To gather data regarding adverse drug reactions (ADRs) along with adverse drug events (ADEs), process reports according to international clinical standards, obtain drug-drug interactions, estimate drug side effects, indicate clinical trials, and incorporate estimation unpredictability toward diagnostic classification algorithms, artificial intelligence (AI) is being utilized for pharmacovigilance and patient safety. The lack of cross-specialty opportunities for training, privacy issues, data models for health care organizations, and insufficient education and training in nations with middle and low incomes are some of the challenges that have been addressed in the context of pharmacology as well as drug safety.

Keywords:  pharmacovigilance,  drug safety,  adverse drug effects,  natural language processing,  XAI,  regulatory aspects,  

## 8.1 Introduction

The Greek word “pharmakon” (meaning drug), as well as the Latin word “vigilare” (meaning to keep watch), are the sources of the term “pharmacovigilance.” Detecting, evaluating, comprehending, and minimizing drug side effects or potential drug-related issues are the scientific and practical endeavors known as “pharmacovigilance” (PV) \[[1](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_001)\]. On a global scale, pharmacovigilance systems are gathering a substantial volume of data daily. Processing the extensive dataset poses a significant challenge. Recently, the program’s scope was extended to include herbals, blood-related products, medical devices, traditional and complementary medicines, herb vigilance, hemovigilance, and materio vigilance \[[2](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_002)\]. Two examples of the unique characteristics of artificial intelligence (AI) are machine learning (ML) and natural language processing (NLP). ML techniques examine structured data, including genetic and imaging data. Unstructured and free-text forms can be distinguished by NLP, an interpretive system that can understand and process human language. AI is also utilized in search engines, clinical settings for treatment, detection, and outcome prediction, and medical diagnosis. With appropriate monitoring, it is also utilized in the healthcare industry to prevent health issues in people \[[3](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_003)\]. Prioritizing patient safety was the primary objective of PV’s design since patients’ exposure to treatment medications throughout clinical trials as well as studies is limited. It allows for the long-term observation and use of drug profiles. It also covers specific populations, including the elderly, racialized, pediatric, and pregnant populations. PV studies are necessary due to the lack of complete data from long-term drug exposure \[[4](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_004)\]. Lifesaving medications like antiretroviral, antitubercular, and anticancer drugs are approved under a fast-track system, making them readily available to patients. PV is in charge of weighing the advantages and disadvantages of these medications.

After a new drug is introduced to the market, drug safety is currently facing significant risks. The main cause of erosion during a clinical trial or post-marketing is the drug’s unpredictable toxicities, which can result in morbidity and mortality even at normal dosages. ADR data must be gathered from pharmaceutical companies by the license holder and submitted to the local drug regulatory body. ADR detection and reporting, technical AE coding, safety individual report preparation, severity assessment, and linkage to suspected drugs are the most crucial tasks in the PV industry. As a result, a new technology is required for the detection of ADRs, as current methods rely on time-consuming human intervention. An AI and ML system was created by a multinational pharmaceutical company and a consulting firm to make it easier to process and maintain quality and compliance standards \[[5](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_005)−7\]. AI and ML are being investigated for their potential to influence PV throughout its whole lifecycle \[[6](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_006), [7](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_007)\]. Furthermore, ML has been used to find complex patterns, or syndromes, in safety data and to perform a range of predictive modeling tasks. Furthermore, pharmacoepidemiology, the study of drug interactions with human populations, is using machine learning (ML) extensively to examine the advantages, drawbacks, and practical applications of drug use \[[8](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_008), [9](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_009)\].

Pharmacovigilance also uses machine learning to assess how well NLP skills classify unstructured text in patient safety reports of incidents. NLP can serve as a layer of protection by identifying cases that result in severe harm or death, based on researchers’ testing of the system’s capacity to classify free text within patient security incidents autonomously and decide the severity of harm outcomes. Several studies looked at the possibility of using machine learning to filter reports on patient safety from sources such as electronic health records \[[10](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_010)\]. Researchers have discovered that near-miss reports gathered by passive surveillance monitoring systems, as well as large, semi-structured, or disorganized collections of adverse events, can be screened and evaluated effectively using text mining and machine learning algorithms \[[11](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_011)\]. A more focused strategy was employed by the researchers, who created a deep-learning model and tested it on various data sets to identify allergic reactions in the free-text narration of hospital safety reports and assess the model’s generalizability \[[12](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_012)\]. According to the study, the model may be applied to enhance allergy treatment, possibly allowing for system enhancement and real-time monitoring events for medical errors. The absence of risk to a patient that can be avoided and the decrease in the possibility of harm coming from receiving medical care are known as patient safety. The healthcare system is integrating AI, which is changing current dynamics (like the role of medical providers) and creating new opportunities to improve patient safety \[[13](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_013), [14](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_014)\].

Due to machine learning, computer systems can now identify latent information and make predictions about the data using both labeled (supervised learning) and unlabeled (unsupervised learning) data, all without the need for explicit programming. Among AI’s many applications, ML, along with natural language processing, stands out for its social impact and frequent use in the medical field \[[15](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_015)\]. Reinforcement learning is the third type of machine learning, where an algorithm learns from its successes and failures as it works to complete a task ([Fig. 8.1](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_fig_001)). Deep learning and artificial neural networks are also included in machine learning. The goal of natural language processing is to enable computers to comprehend human language and convert text into machine-readable, organized information that can be further studied using machine-learning techniques \[[16](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_016), [17](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_017), [18](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_018)\]. Electronic health records (EHRs), which may be gathered from mobile devices and regular medical attention, also contain real-time, practical health data. It might result in a potentially proactive monitoring and safety signal identification strategy. The advent of ML and NLP techniques has created new opportunities for identifying drug safety signals in the free-text EHR data, which is primarily unstructured \[[19](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_019)\].

![Fig. 8.1: Different kinds of ML for the study of pharmacovigilance.](graphic/converted/b_9783111574288-008_fig_001.png)

Fig. 8.1: Different kinds of ML for the study of pharmacovigilance.

## 8.2 Foundation of AI in pharmacovigilance

To improve patient safety and pharmacovigilance, AI is being actively used to collect data on adverse drug reactions (ADRs) and adverse drug events (ADEs), process international clinical standards reports (ICSRs), extract drug-drug interactions and predict their effects, identify high-risk populations for ADRs and inform personalized care, predict drug side effects, portray clinical trials, and incorporate prediction unpredictability into diagnostic classifiers \[[20](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_020)\]. Researchers identified patient diversity and polypharmacy as two potential applications of machine learning for ADR detection \[[21](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_021)\]. Pre-marketing to aftermarket safety evaluations are two stages of the drug development process where these opportunities may arise \[[22](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_022)\].

Automation using machine learning techniques is growing in value due to the variety of disease states, medications, as well as ADRs that patients encounter. A reference standard is compared by certain organizations using machine learning to speed up the utilization of adverse event reports, which is semi-supervised and rule-based \[[23](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_023)\]. Researchers examined and contrasted information posted on social media platforms regarding patient-reported medication outcomes for type 2 diabetes, rheumatoid arthritis, cystic fibrosis, and asthma. While they find that social media platforms are appropriate and complementary sources for analyzing drug outcomes, they also urge greater attention to be paid to developing more dependable techniques for text mining and natural language processing. Furthermore, researchers \[[24](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_024)\] evaluated patient-written reviews of pharmaceutical oncological drugs that were posted to user forums, and they made a comparison between official drug labels and the reported issues. Support vector machines were used to efficiently categorize attitudes toward ADRs. Additionally, other scholars \[[25](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_025)\] looked into the potential of modeling the data exchange among users regarding drug-related therapies for depression using content created by users from a depression-related forum. They discovered that NLP can be used to more precisely identify the ADRs of these medications, and they validated the ADRs with the help of relevant medical literature. Another use of machine learning in pharmacovigilance is evaluating how well NLP can classify unstructured text in patient safety incident reports. Researchers discovered that by identifying cases that cause serious injury or death, NLP is a kind of safety net. To determine the degree of harm outcomes, they assessed how well free text in patient safety incident reports could be automatically classified. However, it is not a perfect method, and it cannot yet take the place of manual review entirely \[[26](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_026)\].

Moreover, XAI can guarantee the interpretability and explainability of AI models by offering a more user-friendly support system to physicians and patients. Along with improving the model’s transparency and traceability, it can also streamline the evaluation process. There is a lot of promise in methodically managing and tracking XAI-based models and working closely with clinicians to improve the system through ongoing fine-tuning that will enable more accurate and efficient clinical decision support delivery. By managing rules, risks, and other requirements, PV XAI’s transparency can also lower the costs associated with PV model governance, cut down on the need for expensive mistakes made during manual inspection, and lower the possibility of unintentional bias \[[27](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_027)\]. Supervised ML learns methods to reduce the gap between its predictions and the (known) reality represented in the labels by using labeled data. Conversely, because patterns appear in high-dimensional spaces and are typically invisible to humans, unsupervised machine learning techniques try to identify them in unlabeled data. Consequently, the labels in supervised learning can reveal whether or not a patient has had an adverse drug reaction in a pharmacovigilance setting, and the resulting model is trained to differentiate individuals \[[28](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_028), [29](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_029)\]. Large volumes of textual data are produced, among other places, on social media, blogs, and forums. Parallel to it, tremendous advancements in artificial intelligence have produced effective NLP techniques and algorithms that make it possible to process and comprehend text produced by humans. It created new avenues for text analysis and social media mining \[[30](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_030)\]. These fields have seen notable innovations in recent years. Text mining is the process of analyzing textual data, such as unstructured or semi-structured text, to find patterns and information hidden within. It thus blends NLP and data mining. The necessity to examine vast volumes of human-language text to extract insights that support data-driven decision-making gave rise to text mining \[[31](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_031), [32](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_032)\].

To improve pharmacovigilance, statistical analysis and machine learning techniques have gained increasing traction in recent years. In a study, two machine learning techniques were used: decision trees as well as inductive logic programming, as shown in [Fig. 8.2,](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_fig_002) to look into common side effect profiles depending on combined drug and target descriptors. Of these, the inductive-logic programming approach is the most effective in relational learning, outperforming decision trees and leveraging not only drug properties, but also past knowledge \[[33](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_033)\]. In a different study, single nucleotide mutations were analyzed using artificial neural networks; however, no significant findings were obtained. Several commonly used algorithms for drug toxicity predictions, including cancer risk, mutagenicity, hepatotoxicity, acute oral toxicities, as well as cardiovascular toxicity, include naive Bayesian, support vector machines, random forests, and back propagation neural networks. These techniques have a well-established theory and are simple to apply \[[34](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_034)\]. Moreover, Naive Bayesian (NB) is a simple Bayesian probabilistic classifier. The feature vectors in the analysis are largely unaffected by the decision variables. Every piece of data needs to be categorized as input, and the output serves as the training sample and characteristic attribute. Each feature attribute for each class of conditional probability estimations is categorized during the classifier training stage, after which the frequency of occurrence of every group in the training sample is calculated. A later classifier is used to categorize the classification items. Using sources of drug information, the following approach has been studied for side-effect prediction \[[35](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_035)\]. It has also been researched for the preclinical safety evaluation of novel medications, as well as for the forecasting of the carcinogenicity, mutagenicity, and hepatotoxicity of various substances. ANNs are inspired by biological neural networks, which use example-based learning to solve problems in the real world \[[36](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_036)\]. It is a paradigm for using highly networked neurons to process information. It is positioned as a scientific and engineering study specialist due to its remarkable capacity to handle imprecise datasets and discover intricate patterns from complex data sets. It has been researched to predict side effects from medication side information \[[37](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_037)\]. By extracting word subsequences that characterize ADRs, the technique has been used to classify them \[[38](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_038)\]. Potential applications have included the analysis of single nucleotide variation changes for drug safety, preclinical safety evaluations of new drugs, and modeling and simulation-based prediction of the carcinogenicity and mutagenicity of chemicals \[[39](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_039), [40](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_040), [41](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_041), [42](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_042)\].

![Fig. 8.2: Various AI-based tools for pharmacovigilance and drug safety.](graphic/converted/b_9783111574288-008_fig_002.png)

Fig. 8.2: Various AI-based tools for pharmacovigilance and drug safety.

Representational learning, which generates predictive features from data, is a basic concept in machine learning. The process lessens the need for the laborious, sometimes necessary, human feature engineering found in non-ML techniques. One method for representational learning that can be enhanced is regression. Non-ML AI expressly uses symbolic representations of human reasoning and coded domain knowledge. These analyses can be regarded as falling outside the purview of artificial intelligence in pharmacovigilance (AIPV) because they initially relied on static rules that were unable to self-correct or self-learn \[[43](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_043)\]. However, AI in general, and AIPV in particular, are still evolving, if unfortunately, sometimes in lockstep with hype cycles. The twenty-first century is seeing a resurgence of sorts for non-ML symbolic AI, so it’s important not to write it off entirely. There are two illustrative examples. First of all, description logic is a web-based system for knowledge representation that enables data to be analyzed and read by machines \[[44](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_044)\]. Better adverse event cases and queries, in addition to data set integration and inferencing, are made easier by the language. These elements matter in many different PV domains. Second, hybrid methods combine symbolic AI and deep learning to enhance each other. One example of this is neurosymbolic AI. These methods are currently being used in PV-related fields \[[45](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_045)\].

## 8.3 Challenges and opportunities of AI in pharmacovigilance and drug safety

In LMICs, there is a dearth of PV healthcare professionals (HCPs) as a result of inadequate training and educational opportunities. Professionals in a traditional PV system operate in four domains: qualified persons for pharmacovigilance (QPPV), systems, operations, and surveillance. Traditional PV requires QPPVs, data/system security administrators, drug safety physicians, and supervisors of drug safety. But for AI-based PV, specialists in a variety of AI domains are also required. For example, machine learning, as well as natural language processing (NLP) algorithm development, requires engineers. It is yet another significant barrier for LMICs. Very few LMICs are among the very few nations that provide cross-specialty, even cross-language learning for AI-based PV. Although AI technologies have already been used in pharmacovigilance systems, there are currently very few dedicated training programs for AI-based pharmacovigilance systems available globally. The lack of funding for healthcare professionals (HCPs) from low- and middle-income countries may also restrict the opportunities for HCPs to attend these courses. Despite this, HCPs in developed nations could receive training in AI technologies like data mining, deep learning, and natural language processing. However, there aren’t many options for AI training in LMICs \[[46](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_046)\].

Every medical facility supports a different data model than the others, and every kind of data model has the potential to be distinct with its own set of terminologies and value representation. Across several institutions, data from disparate sources must be combined and transformed into a format that is compatible with the common data model (CDM). To do this, it will take time and effort for engineers and HCPs to transform the data. Because algorithms and training models in AI-based PV rely on data with annotations to make predictions on unannotated data, data annotation is crucial. The high cost of annotation and the dearth of excellent quality data for model training inhibit research on AI-based PV. Many ICSRs can be found in structured databases, but these databases are unable to recognize possible PV signals \[[47](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_047)\]. The availability of structured as well as curated data for software training to detect possible drug safety issues is one of the primary obstacles to AI adoption. Privacy concerns are also raised by using AI in pharmacovigilance because participant consent may not be obtained before data is used for other purposes, as shown in [Fig. 8.3](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_fig_003) \[[48](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_048)\].

Everyone is entitled to privacy and the safeguarding of their private medical data. Furthermore, laws are in place to safeguard the privacy of sensitive information that can be used to identify a specific person on a regional and national level. It is necessary for people working in the PV system to have specific knowledge and abilities \[[49](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_049), [50](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_050)\]. These structures are currently constructed using tried-and-true methods, but conventional PV and IT interfaces become disrupted when machine learning is applied, for example. Existing evidence suggests that artificial intelligence has the potential to match or even surpass human performance in a variety of medical science domains. Change management involves individuals and functions that are affected by the change \[[51](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_051), [52](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_052)\]. The PV system spans many domains beyond the safety division of the MAH, and the introduction of new IT will have an impact on numerous stakeholders both upstream and downstream \[[53](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_053), [54](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_053)\]. The implementation of such a system will probably bring about unanticipated difficulties, but it will also bring about unanticipated opportunities to enhance the PV system throughout its entirety. Intelligent automation’s effects on business continuity planning are significant. As automation increases, it might be wise to concentrate on starting up automated processes again and stop using manual workarounds. However, to follow PV regulations and maintain vigilance during a disruption to safeguard patient safety, it will be crucial to remain adaptable \[[54](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_054), [55](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_055), [56](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_056)\]. Furthermore, distinguishing between causal and associative relationships using observational data in the presence of confounding variables (i.e., variables related to both exposure and outcome) and colliders (i.e., factors that are affected by both exposure and outcome) presents a particularly significant challenge when incorporating machine learning. Although a multivariable regression approach is frequently utilized to account for the possibility of confounding factors, it is not possible to directly estimate the effects of causality. In addition, before causal connections can be established, observational studies must be conducted to capture and evaluate temporal relationships \[[57](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_057), [58](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_058)\].

Consequently, it is believed to present a chance to apply causal inference paradigms to the main machine-learning issues that the pharmacovigilance industry is currently dealing with. Examples of causal study designs, including Granger causality, hypothetical reasoning, as well as do-calculus, are less commonly used in pharmacovigilance tasks. It is especially important to investigate these types of study designs. In addition, there is a dearth of research that investigates the possibility of addressing the fairness problem in machine learning by incorporating causal paradigms. It could be a new path for pharmacovigilance studies to pursue \[[59](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_059)\]. Even though organizations in the life sciences are interested in AI and ML, they are concerned that ML and AI would not produce the same results as traditional case processing. Because they are unable to fully comprehend the potential that automated PV reporting systems could bring to their operations, many businesses in the health sciences industry struggle to define demanding requirements for their developers and to carefully evaluate vendors and platforms that are available on the market. Because they are unsure of the full potential of the platform, they may choose to limit their forecast and instead choose or develop solutions that adhere to established procedures rather than seeking room to reconsider \[[60](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_060)\]. Even though it may only have a moderately favorable output, it will result in a significant reduction in the amount of time and money spent on pharmacovigilance operations, as well as an improvement in the quality of the results. Pharmaceutical companies can hire technology experts rather than industry experts, only to find out that these partners are not knowledgeable about the complex rules and regulations that govern international pharmacovigilance practices, even though they have almost adopted an existing platform for these kinds of applications. Furthermore, the extent to which developing and impoverished nations engage may be hindered by challenges such as limited accessibility, reliability, insufficient expertise in handling, and acceptance of technological use”. \[[61](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_061)\].

![Fig. 8.3: Various challenges and solutions of AI in pharmacovigilance and drug safety.](graphic/converted/b_9783111574288-008_fig_003.png)

Fig. 8.3: Various challenges and solutions of AI in pharmacovigilance and drug safety.

## 8.4 Collaboration and partnership for drug safety

An agreement between the public and private sectors that is cooperative is known as a “public-private partnership,” or PPP. In this arrangement, the two sectors work together to accomplish a goal that is compatible or mutually beneficial by sharing resources, risks, and duties. The premise of public-private partnerships (PPPs) is founded on the conviction that, from the bench to the bedside, neither the public nor the private sectors can successfully monitor and sustain innovation across an extended period on their own \[[62](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_062)\]. A research and development procedure created exclusively for the public sector could be excessively sluggish and ineffectual. The ability of the public and private sectors to work together and share resources, including financial resources, knowledge, data, samples, medical knowledge, patient base, facilities, risks, and rewards, is one of the main benefits of a public-private partnership (PPP). More importantly, they create wider networks of experts with diverse viewpoints. In addition to making the innovation process more efficient, cost-effective, and time-bound, a public-private partnership (PPP) can also help to facilitate and improve the overall fluidity and holistic nature of the research and growth process \[[63](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_063)\]. The private sector routinely uses medications at a substantially higher frequency than the public sector does. Adverse drug reactions, or ADRs, are one of the leading causes of death and are predicted to become a major public health concern in the not-too-distant future \[[64](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_064), [65](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_065)\]. In both urban and rural areas, private physicians are the only providers of healthcare, according to the findings of the National Sample Survey Office (NSSO). Furthermore, due to the private pharmaceutical industry’s explosive growth, the private sector urgently needs handholding support to overcome obstacles like the underreporting of ADRs, the creation of a national patient safety database, as well as the pursuit of mutual benefits like recognition and avoiding regulatory action \[[66](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_066), [67](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_067), [68](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_068)\]. Pharmacovigilance requires the involvement of healthcare professionals to be successful. Consequently, a strong system and tools for the tracking, reporting, and assessment of ADRs have been established as a result of the collaboration between the PvPI and academic medical institutions, public and private hospitals \[[69](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_069), [70](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_070)\]. Consequently, the PvPI tackles the problems by informing and arguing with the hospital administration and the medical staff that reporting ADRs is entirely voluntary and does not result in disciplinary action. Reports obtained from the public sector even have higher quality completion scores than those from the private sector. The private sector’s reports have a low overall rating because they withhold information about alternative dispute resolutions out of fear of lawsuits and a desire to maintain confidentiality \[[71](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_071)\].

Historically, the primary goal of NRAs has been to collaborate with healthcare providers and the industry to report passive ADRs through hard copy and online reporting channels. NRAs are essential regulators in the field of pharmacovigilance. Pharmacovigilance has looked into the possibilities of using artificial intelligence and keeping electronic health records over the last few years \[[72](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_072)\]. More people are coming to understand and accept the idea that patients and caregivers should be involved in the process of creating and regulating health products. Since they will ultimately be using the products in the healthcare industry, patients should be educated, engaged, and treated as participants in their care. They also possess the greatest depth of knowledge regarding their perspectives, experiences, and insights. Therefore, the viewpoints of patients and their life experiences can help to improve a health product at different stages of its life cycle \[[73](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_073)\]. Regulatory approvals, post-approval monitoring, clinical trial designs, research priorities, and technology evaluations are a few of these stages. The involvement of patients in pharmaceutical surveillance is crucial because it provides more context than the reports that healthcare providers present. One illustration of this would be the simple fact that patient reports usually include a more thorough description, especially when it comes to the impact on daily life, which may not be fully understood by medical professionals. It enhances patient participation in their care, adds to the reports provided by healthcare providers, and raises consciousness about ADRs \[[74](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_074), [75](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_075)\].

In the digital age, patients can now more easily report ADRs due to online reporting via the Internet and smartphones \[[76](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_076)\]. In the aforementioned French study, which examined the variables influencing patient participation in pharmacovigilance observations and the development of patient involvement, it was found that the availability of digital solutions affected the rise in patient reporting of adverse drug reactions \[[77](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_077)\]. In the field of drug safety communication, which is a vital part of pharmacovigilance, a further significant collaborative partnership among patients is essential. A study that examined the pharmacovigilance environments in eight ASEAN nations discovered that a common problem is communication \[[78](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_078)\]. This is even though open and honest communication about the advantages, drawbacks, and efficacy of medications increases the likelihood of safe medication use among patients. For the sake of the general public’s health, information regarding drug safety must be made accessible, easily accessed, as well as successfully communicated.

## 8.5 Regulatory aspects of AI in pharmacovigilance and drug safety

AI holds a great deal of promise for revolutionizing the pharmaceutical industry, even though its application in pharmaceutical regulatory affairs continues to be in its infancy. Through the dissemination of information regarding the most recent developments in the industry, artificial intelligence can be of assistance in the management of regulatory affairs. This is especially true regarding the potential advantages of AI for regulatory approval as well as drug discovery. The term “regulatory affairs” refers to the groups or departments within pharmaceutical companies that act as a conduit to regulatory bodies, both nationally and globally, to obtain product approval in accordance with the laws and regulations that are currently in place. In turn, regulatory agencies give their approval to products based on the information and documentation that is provided throughout the entire process of drug discovery along with product development \[[79](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_079)\]. The Central Drugs Standard Control Organization (CDSCO) mandates that Marketing Authorization Holders (MAHs) report the Individual Case Safety Reports (ICSR) of any drug marketed in India to both the National Coordination Center for Pharmacovigilance Program of India (NCC-PvPI) and the MAHs themselves. The management and handling of these documents are becoming more costly, time-consuming, and laborious due to the increasing volume of ICSR reports. Therefore, to cut down on the time, resources, and labor needed for processing ICSR cases, PvPI may also use machine intelligence techniques like artificial intelligence \[[80](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_080)\]. Regarding the acquisition of precise data, the use of AI technologies in pharmacovigilance (PV) is very advantageous. Almost all aspects of PV in handling cases and risk tracking can be automated or made easier with the help of AI tools, which contributes to a reduction in the overall processing time. One idea that has been proposed is that of Beneficial AI, which is artificial intelligence developed in a secure manner and for the benefit of society. To construct AI that is beneficial, two different types of measures have been proposed. Because of extrinsic measures, artificial intelligence designers are required to adopt beneficial designs. Including certain requirements, incentives, and constraints is one way that these designs could be implemented. Measures that are deemed intrinsic include the formation of social norms and the organization of communications. Extrinsic measures may be impacted by the influence of intrinsic measures \[[81](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_081)\].

Furthermore, artificial intelligence technologies in PV greatly aid in the accurate extraction of information processes. Almost all PV case processing and risk tracking tasks can be automated or simplified with the use of AI tools, which helps to shorten processing times overall \[[82](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_082)\]. The PV database known as VigiBase stores the information in a format that is both structured and ordered, making it possible to easily analyze the data that has been recorded. The WHO International Classification of Diseases, the WHO Drug Classification, and the WHO-Adverse Reaction Terminology/Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA) are some of the medical and drug classifications linked to the system. VigiBase is a web application that the general public can use to browse and easily access information on the negative effects of drugs \[[83](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_083)\]. In addition, VigiLyze is an online resource that offers a clear and concise overview of VigiBase, which can be investigated online for more information \[[84](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_084)\]. VigiGrade, an online ICSR management system, is employed for worldwide drug surveillance. It does so by gathering, preparing, and disseminating data to enable efficient data analysis. MedDRA and WHO Drug support the system \[[85](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_085)\]. As a result, VigiMatch is an algorithm that finds individual case reports that are similar to one another by using probabilistic matching of patterns. It is significant to remember that the main objective of VigiGrade is to evaluate the degree of completeness of clinically relevant data in an organized manner on an individual’s case report. It is carried out as part of the process of informing nations about the caliber of the data \[[86](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_086)\].

The various regulatory bodies are transitioning from paper-based submissions to electronic submissions of the CTD. CTD (paper-based) submissions have been stopped from being accepted by several different authorities. It has been determined to take action to expedite the submission process and centralize the procedure. Regulations worldwide have been greatly impacted by the switch from paper to electronic submission of Common Technical Documents (CTD) \[[87](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_087), [88](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_088)\]. The acceptance of submissions in the electronic submission of the CTD format has resulted in the optimization of resources, the increase of efficiency, and the improvement of application tracking. Regulations worldwide have been greatly impacted by the switch from paper to electronic submission of Common Technical Documents (CTD). The Food and Drug Administration (FDA) in the United States has implemented requirements for the submission of electronic clinical trial data (eCTD) for a variety of applications, such as biologics license applications, new drug applications, as well as abbreviated new drug applications. There has been a positive impact on regulatory procedures as a result of the implementation of electronic submission of CTDs \[[89](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_089)\]. Individuals who have experience with electronic document submission (eCTD) can show financial savings over paper submissions and shorten the time it takes for their submissions to be approved. This phenomenon has been noted in a broad range of organizations, regardless of the magnitude or volume of submitted work. In addition, the utilization of electronic submission and review (eCTD) has made the process of reviewing and managing the lifecycle of electronic submissions easier, which has resulted in the harmonization of electronic submission and review initiatives \[[90](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_090), [91](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_091)\].

Due to their effects on drug research, medical practice, and regulatory professionals’ responsibilities, each of these trends has a variety of related issues that need to be addressed. Ethical considerations encompass a wide range of topics, such as privacy, wellbeing, and the preservation of human autonomy \[[91](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_091)\]. It is crucial to ensure transparency and allow capability explanation when it comes to deep learning models that are used in machine learning and artificial intelligence. Furthermore, these technologies must be utilized in a responsible and accountable manner. The emergence of digital disruption presents notable obstacles for enterprises seeking to improve workforce competencies and comprehension via employee upskilling and reskilling programs. Implementing a mentorship program, in which experienced employees offer advice and support to less experienced colleagues during their transition period, is one possible way to address these issues \[[92](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_092), [93](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_093), [94](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_094)\]. Moreover, India’s National Pharmacovigilance Program was launched in November 2004 by the Central Drugs Standard Control Organization to improve drug safety through the collection and assessment of adverse drug reaction data. Using a tripartite structure and multiple hubs expedites the implementation of regulations to guarantee public health. Its main objective is to promote a notification culture among doctors and other medical staff members, like nurses and pharmacists. The program permits reporting of common or surface-level side effects of even established drugs, even though drug regulators would be more intrigued to learn about adverse drug reactions of recently marketed drugs \[[95](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_095)\]. The purpose of it is to incentivize all healthcare providers to begin disclosing adverse events. The reporting forms protect the privacy of the patient. Nonetheless, to facilitate information verification and deter the submission of false information, notification identification is required. Peripheral Pharmacovigilance Centers can now be established by licensed medical professionals, including pharmacists \[[96](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_096)\]. Pharmacovigilance (PV) systems of today handle safety data using algorithmic techniques based on binary logic. These user-managed algorithms use standardized, objective expert knowledge to ensure that users are familiar with and understand the results of data processing. The reporting forms protect the privacy of the patient. Nonetheless, to facilitate information verification and deter the submission of false information, notification identification is required. Peripheral Pharmacovigilance Centers can now be established by licensed medical professionals, including pharmacists \[[97](#9783111573861_011894.xhtml#b_9783111574288-008_ref_097)\].

## 8.6 Conclusion

Pharmacovigilance (PV) is a scientific and applied endeavor that attempts to identify, evaluate, understand, and minimize the negative effects of drugs or potential drug-related issues. Natural language processing and machine learning are two distinct features of AI used in web searches, clinical settings, treatment, surveillance, outcome prediction, and medical diagnosis. Data intake, anomaly detection, and duplicate detection have all benefited from AI/ML. To gather information on ADEs and ADRs, process reports according to international clinical standards, obtain drug-drug interactions, predict adverse reactions to drugs, represent clinical trials, and incorporate estimation unpredictability into diagnostic classification algorithms, AI is being utilized for pharmacovigilance and patient safety. Machine learning techniques are becoming more and more useful for predicting medication side effects, identifying high-risk populations for adverse drug reactions, and incorporating prediction unpredictability into diagnostic classifiers. A developing area in pharmacovigilance is text mining, which combines NLP and data mining. The classification of ADRs, side-effect prediction, and drug toxicity prediction are all done with artificial neural networks (ANNs). Nevertheless, there are obstacles in the way of pharmacovigilance and drug safety, including the scarcity of opportunities for cross-specialty training, privacy concerns, and data models for healthcare facilities, as well as limited training and education in LMICs. PPPs, or public-private partnerships, can aid in resolving these problems and enhancing the creative process.

# Chapter 9 Intellectual property and data privacy in the pharmaceutical sector

#### Abstract

In the pharmaceutical sector, data privacy is essential to providing quality care. However, protecting large volumes of data has proven to be a difficult mandate, and the healthcare sector is still vulnerable to data breaches that make headlines. Intellectual property rights (IPR) are crucial to the pharmaceutical industry because they allow inventors to legally maintain the confidentiality of their inventions for a predetermined amount of time. The exclusive rights that inventors and creators have to make money off their inventions are known as intellectual property rights or IPRs. These rights are protected under various laws, including patents, copyrights, and trademarks. The chapter also discusses how blockchain technology can be used to manage IPRs and improve patient privacy. It highlights the challenges of data privacy in the pharmaceutical industry, including cybersecurity risks and compliance with data protection laws. In the intricate pharmaceutical supply chain, cooperation between distributors, manufacturers, and other stakeholders is essential for safeguarding data privacy. Overall, the chapter emphasizes the necessity of a balanced approach to data privacy and intellectual property, as well as the intricate and regulated nature of the pharmaceutical industry. It suggests that addressing unfair practices and promoting innovation can contribute to the welfare of consumers and the advancement of society.

Keywords:  data privacy,  copyright,  patent,  trademark,  authentication,  encryption,  cybersecurity,  

## 9.1 Introduction

In most economies, the pharmaceutical industry is highly regulated and complicated. The use of “learned intermediaries” such as physicians and pharmacists, restrictions on advertising and promotion, public and private insurance programs, and government purchasing and price controls all have a major impact on the demand for pharmaceuticals. Competition is shaped equally by strict product safety reviews, manufacturing regulatory oversight, and legal frameworks governing the transfer of technology between commercial entities and publicly funded biomedical research institutions on the supply side \[[1](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_001)\]. All original works of human intellect, whether they are in the arts, sciences, technology, or literature, are considered intellectual property (IP). The legal entitlement of a creator or inventor to maintain the confidentiality of their idea or innovation for a specified duration is referred to as innovation for a specified duration is referred (IPRs) \[[2](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_002)\]. For a specified duration, these legal rights confer onto the inventor or their assignee the exclusive authority to exploit their invention. The importance of intellectual property in the modern economic environment has been acknowledged for an extended period. Furthermore, it has been clearly established that the intellectual effort behind the discovery deserves recognition for its contributions to the public welfare. Research and development (R&D) expenditures have escalated, leading to a corresponding increase in the investments necessary to introduce innovative technology to the market \[[3](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_003)\].

Europe is where the rules and regulations about intellectual property originated. The fourteenth century marked the inception of the patent-granting tendency. England exhibited superior scientific advancement in specific domains compared to other European nations, hence enticing immigrant workers under advantageous conditions. Italy is the origin of recognized copyrights. The majority of legal thought that resulted in the first laws and legal systems in history, which eventually spread to other countries, occurred in Venice, which is why the city is recognized as the birthplace of the intellectual property system \[[4](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_004)\]. In general, multinational corporations controlled the Indian medicine market in the post-independence era. India was among the most expensive countries in the world for drug prices, and the cost of the drugs was higher when they were imported. The committee correctly noted that a clear recommendation for guaranteeing the food, medicine, and medical device needs of the Indian population should be included in the patent act. These ought to be made as inexpensively available to the general public as feasible, with the patent holder receiving just compensation. In 1957, the Indian government took two significant actions: first, it formed Hindustan Antibiotics Limited through a UNICEF agreement; second, it designated the Justice Rajagopala Ayyangar Committee to amend the patent law and establish a market that could be sustained locally \[[5](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_005), [6](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_006), [7](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_007)\].

IPRs are typically regarded as having two principal areas of impact within the pharmaceutical sector. The primary concerns are the pricing and availability issues, namely the interaction between IPRs, particularly patent rights, and the cost and accessibility of innovative medications. The function of IPRs in providing incentives for the development, commercialization, and discovery of innovative pharmaceuticals is the second issue, namely research and development (R&D) incentives. Furthermore, IPRs influence research and development expenditures and their allocation among diseases, countries, and organizations. The two concerns are intricately connected, and their interplay presents numerous formidable policy and economic challenges \[[8](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_008)\]. Utilizing IPRs to effectively reconcile “static” customer advantages from reduced prices and a competitive medicine supply with “dynamic” benefits from breakthrough products presents significant challenges, even within the confines of a single nation. On the one hand, declining effective patent life and growing R&D costs are severely pressuring industry finances. However, despite the enormous positive effects that pharmaceutical innovation has on the economy and public health, even in wealthy nations, the high cost of over-the-counter medications often prompts contentious political debates about issues of access and equity for underprivileged or low-income populations, as well as how best to allocate public health care funds \[[9](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_009)\].

The present condition of the pharmaceutical sector indicates the inequitable promotion and exploitation of IPRs to the detriment of competition and customer welfare. The inequity affecting societal welfare is underscored by the drug industry’s deficiency in innovation and reluctance to embrace risk. It is an injustice that cannot be remedied by mere legislative reform. Although legislative initiatives aimed at addressing deficiencies in existing statutes and enacting new regulations to further curtail the pharmaceutical industry’s inequitable business practices may offer some respite, antitrust law must effectively intervene \[[10](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_010)\]. Antitrust laws have rigorously examined certain corporate activities in the pharmaceutical sector, including non-competition agreements and mergers and acquisitions; however, numerous additional practices require attention. Antitrust law can contribute to maintaining equilibrium between incentivizing innovation and safeguarding competition in various domains, such as the issuance of patents for minor elements of obsolete pharmaceuticals, reformulating drugs to secure new patents, and employing advertising and brand expansion to elevate barriers for generic rivals \[[11](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_011)\].

Consequently, in the wake of globalization, it is essential to stay innovative and creatively ahead of the curve to compete in the competitive trade and technology markets. India is renowned for its intellectual prowess in a variety of technological domains, including software engineering, missile technology, missions to the Moon or Jupiter, and other areas \[[12](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_012), [13](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_013), [14](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_014)\]. IPRs and the framework of its policies have a direct impact on any society’s development. IPRs ignorance leads to the demise of inventions, increased infringement risk, financial loss, and the end of the nation’s intellectual era. Therefore, to support domestic inventions and advancements in the field of research and technology, there is an urgent need to disseminate information about IPRs \[[15](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_015), [16](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_016), [17](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_017)\].

## 9.2 Intellectual property rights landscape

IPRs refer to thoughts, creations, and artistic works that the public recognizes as proprietary. IPRs confer exclusive rights to owners, enabling them to monetize their inventions or reputations. A variety of laws, such as those about patents, copyrights, trademarks, and others, protect intellectual property as represented in [Fig. 9.1](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_fig_001).

### 9.2.1 The patent

A product or technique that presents a novel solution to an old problem or a new technical approach is called an invention. An invention is entitled to an exclusive patent, which gives the patent holder protection for their invention. This protection is implemented for a limited duration. A patented innovation cannot be manufactured, utilized, sold, or disseminated for profit without the consent of the patent holder. The patent holder is responsible for determining who may utilize the innovation and under what conditions while it is protected by the patent. Patent owners may license third parties to utilize the invention under mutually acceptable conditions. The owner may transfer the rights to the invention to a third party, who will then assume ownership of the patent. Upon the expiration of a patent, an invention forfeits its exclusive rights and becomes available for economic exploitation by others, thus concluding its protection \[[18](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_018)\]. The phrase “invention entering the public domain” denotes this concept. The majority of nations have enacted domestic laws to safeguard their IPRs. The protection granted to inventors and creators in specific countries, such as India, or regions, such as the European Union, is localized and cannot be transferred to other countries or regions, with the exception of copyrights \[[19](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_019)\]. A patent granted in India is valid just within that jurisdiction and cannot be enforced in the USA. The primary motive for patenting an idea is to generate profit from its exclusivity. The resultant patent would confer exclusive market rights to the patent owner if the inventor or their assignee: (a) developed a significant invention after considering customer modifications; and (b) the invention was precisely articulated and claimed in the patent specification prepared by the patent agent. The patent holder may exercise their exclusive rights by selling the patented invention directly to consumers or by licensing it to another entity \[[20](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_020)\].

Furthermore, a multinational accord referred to as the Patent Cooperation Treaty (PCT) was enacted in 1978. By specifying the countries of interest in the PCT application, an inventor from a PCT contracting state may concurrently get priority for their invention in any or all member nations. It obviates the necessity for the inventor to file a distinct application in each member nation \[[21](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_021)\]. To protect an innovation internationally, a distinct patent application must be filed in each pertinent country; in some circumstances, it must occur within a designated timeframe to get priority in those jurisdictions. A substantial and prompt financial investment is necessary to address filing fees, translation costs, legal charges, and other related expenditures. Moreover, the limited timeframe may render conclusions regarding the necessity of filing a patent application in a country misguided \[[22](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_022)\].

### 9.2.2 The copyright

The term “copyright” refers to the legal protection afforded to published works, which prohibits anyone other than the author from publishing or selling them. A set of exclusive rights known as copyright governs how an idea or piece of information is used in a specific way. In its broadest sense, it refers to “the right to copy” an original work. Literary works such as plays, novels, poems, and reference books, as well as technical drawings, maps, and advertisements, as well as computer programs, databases, movies, musical compositions, photographs, sculptures, and architecture, are a few examples of works that are covered by copyright. Works that are the product of human intelligence and creativity are protected by copyright. Both published and unpublished works are included. Expressions of ideas are protected by copyright, not the ideas themselves. For a predetermined amount of time, it permits the copyright holder to prevent others from reproducing the work \[[23](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_023)\]. As it gives creators of content exclusive rights over their original works, copyright law is crucial. Among other things, it offers protection for works of art, music, literature, and film. Authors can manage how their works are reproduced, distributed, performed in public, and altered due to copyright laws. Content creators are better equipped to protect their works when they are aware of the extent of copyright protection, including the duration of rights, the requirements for copyright registration, and the notion of fair use \[[24](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_024)\].

A multitude of research initiatives have been conducted across different countries to examine copyright legislation from diverse perspectives. The study’s author focused on copyright and public domain legislation. The study indicates that the public domain is a fundamental component of copyright law, and its incorporation meets both historical and logical criteria. Without citing “public domain copyright,” one cannot justify their actions. The study goes on to say that copyright and public domain are similar in that they have similar evaluative values. According to a related study, the general public is a stakeholder in copyrights. According to the author, the US copyright law grants creators several exclusive rights, many of whom either knowingly or unknowingly give up these rights to business entities \[[25](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_025)\]. Citizens should support a copyright system that considers their interests since the general public is now considered to be a stakeholder in copyright. Traditionally vested interests must come first. The ethical and financial aspects of copyright were demonstrated by three additional studies on copyright policy. The term of protection was the subject of one study. It concluded that extending the protection would be detrimental and needless. According to the second study, there are two different schools of thought on copyright law: the first emphasizes the creators’ inherent rights to the money made from their works, while the second highlights the high cost of creating a framework for copyright that maximizes social welfare. The study’s findings ultimately demonstrated that the “natural rights view” had a stronger impact on Canada’s copyright laws than the economic perspective \[[26](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_026)\].

### 9.2.3 The trademark

Article 15 of the TRIPS Agreement stipulates that “any sign, or any combination of signs, capable of distinguishing the goods or services of one enterprise from those of others, is necessary to constitute a trademark.” Due to industrialization, the trademark has gained prominence in contemporary international trade \[[27](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_027)\]. A trademark is a unique symbol or logo that signifies the manufacture or provision of a specific product by an individual, organization, or economic sector. Service marks, akin to trademarks, serve to differentiate service providers from their competitors. A business may employ various types of trademarks for distinct items, while trade names serve to differentiate it from competing enterprises. Trade names and trademarks assist companies in establishing their brand, enhancing awareness, and fostering trust with consumers. Consumers typically depend on trademarks due to the challenges associated with swiftly evaluating a product or service to determine its quality \[[28](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_028)\]. Any distinguishable mark, or one that can be graphically represented and used to differentiate the goods and services of one company from another, is eligible for trademark registration under the Indian Trademarks Act. Trademarks don’t grant exclusive rights that could be abused, so their validity shouldn’t be restricted. However, if there were no time limit on trademark validity, there would be an excessive number of registered trademarks that have no use \[[29](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_029)\].

### 9.2.4 The trade secret

A trade secret is any knowledge that is kept confidential, not widely known to the public, and gives its owner a financial or competitive advantage. Trade secrets have a high value – tens or even hundreds of millions of dollars – and large damage awards have been given in trade secret lawsuits. A trade secret is misappropriated when it is obtained through “improper means.” Nonetheless, if the claimed trade secret was created independently, became known to the public, or was not kept a secret, then those defenses may be able to successfully contest a claim of misappropriation of trade secrets. Trade secrets are not as well known as other IPRs, even though they are an intrinsically valuable intangible asset for any business \[[30](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_030)\]. Its unpopularity is attributed to numerous factors: First off, registration is not necessary for trade secrets. Trade secrets are generally credited with developing and being preserved due to standard business practices and business ethics. Even though trade secret laws are based on evolving legal theories, there is currently a weak enforcement system. Third, disputes involving such secrecy are seldom open to public discourse, implying that access to information regarding such matters is usually limited, rendering trade secrets comparatively unpopular. Fourth, trade secrets do not grant their owner the sole right to utilize the information, in contrast to other types of intellectual property. The right may belong to others when knowledge is produced independently or obtained through the process of reverse engineering. Trade secrets provide the business with several financial advantages \[[31](#9783111573861_013307.xhtml#b_9783111574288-009_ref_031)\]. Ensuring appropriate rewards for any invention that is supported by appropriate trade secret protection gives the company financial stability. To prevent breaches, no expensive system needs to be set up. Moreover, it always worries about knowledge spillovers leading to the misuse of company information. Trade secrets strike a balance between appropriate disclosures and secrecy, even though they rely on reasonable methods to maintain confidentiality. As a result, trade secrets may lead to independent invention or knowledge diffusion. The non-exclusivity restriction strikes the perfect balance between maintaining the essential distinction from absolute secrecy and ensuring confidentiality.
