# Chapter 13 Future trends and innovations of AI in healthcare

#### Abstract

Artificial intelligence (AI)is an important technological advancement that has allowed humans to replace manual labor at greater psychological and logical capacities in various industries, beyond the physical labor promoted by machines. The chapter emphasizes the future trends and other innovations present in AI in the healthcare system. AI has advanced quickly in recent years along is now being applied to offer many benefits in the crucial healthcare industry. An overview of AI’s current status in the medical and healthcare fields is given in the chapter, with an emphasis on the technology’s future. AI is now being used to provide numerous advantages in the vital healthcare sector, particularly in the areas of early detection and diagnostics. With future health care delivered through the H-IoT system being a reality owing to the development of vital methods that the scientific community has assisted in paving the way for, the state of the technology today offers countless opportunities for enhancement and innovation. AI is now being used to provide numerous advantages in the vital healthcare sector, especially in the areas of early detection and diagnostics. AI will be utilized in smarter hospitals and patient-physician interactions in the future of smart-connected healthcare (SCH).

Keywords:  innovation,  Healthify,  Dehealth,  HintVR,  bump galaxy,  health blocks,  AccuVein,  

## 13.1 Introduction

Technology has advanced significantly since the Industrial Revolution and continues to be the primary driver of growth and production. Technological developments in machines that have substituted manual labor-intensive jobs have led to significant progress in human development. Artificial intelligence (AI) is an important advancement in technology that has allowed humans to take on physical labor at bigger mental and reasonable capacities across industries, beyond the manual support provided by machines \[[1](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_001)\]. AI is a field of research and technological advances that allows intelligent machines and software applications to perform tasks that typically require human intelligence. Consequently, one of AI’s primary advantages is its capacity to replicate human behavior in a variety of contexts, learn from errors, and adapt to new ones. Big Data and other relevant data sources are used by AI to improve performance on tasks. Among the numerous sectors that have profited from AI recent rapid development is the crucial healthcare sector \[[2](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_002)\]. AI has digitally replaced manual health systems with automated ones in many areas. In some applications, managing patients and medical resources basic tasks is now all that is required of humans, with AI components handling more complex procedures or leaving them to their own devices. AI-powered healthcare systems are evolving swiftly, particularly in the fields of diagnosis and early detection as shown in [Fig. 13.1](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_fig_001) \[[3](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_003)\].

![Fig. 13.1: Schematic representation of ML algorithm for hot spot prediction.](graphic/converted/b_9783111574288-013_fig_001.png)

Fig. 13.1: Schematic representation of ML algorithm for hot spot prediction.

AI improves performance on a variety of tasks by utilizing relevant information sources. AI has advanced quickly in the past few years and has been used to great effect across numerous industries, including the vital healthcare sector. The healthcare industry is one of those that has benefited greatly from AI. In many fields, AI has already electronically transformed the healthcare industry into a computerized version \[[4](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_004), [5](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_005)\]. As a result, in certain programs, human intervention is limited to basic medical tasks like managing patients and medical resources; complex operations are either fully performed by humans or by AI components. Healthcare systems driven by AI are developing swiftly, particularly in the areas of early detection and diagnosis. As a result, AI is now able to complete tasks that are challenging for humans to complete with the same effectiveness, dependability, meticulousness, and speed as AI at a lower cost \[[6](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_006), [7](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_007), [8](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_008), [9](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_009)\]. If AI developers are successful in creating systems that can carry out particular tasks, the technological advances enabled by the digitization of healthcare may be able to assist in overcoming other obstacles. AI, for instance, has a chance to dramatically reduce healthcare expenses while also enhancing patient care. The increasing number of people on the planet is predicted to result in a greater need for healthcare to be delivered more quickly. Because of this, the medical sector requires innovative AI solutions to increase productivity and effectiveness without driving up expenses \[[10](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_010)\].

By using relevant data sources, AI enhances performance on a variety of tasks. AI has advanced quickly in the past few years and has been used to great effect across numerous industries, including the vital healthcare sector. The healthcare sector has benefited immensely from AI \[[11](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_011)\]. AI has electronically transformed many areas of the healthcare system into a computerized version. Therefore, complex processes are either managed by AI components in some programs, or they are handled by humans for the most part, with humans only being required to handle basic medical practice tasks like managing clients along with medical resources. Healthcare systems driven by AI are rapidly developing, particularly in early detection and diagnostics \[[12](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_012)\].

More creative and ingenious solutions will be used to improve patient-physician interactions as a result of smart-connected healthcare, or SCH. Mobile apps will enable the network to hyperlink geographically to the closest hospital in the event of an accident and transfer data before admission, potentially saving lives \[[13](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_013)\]. It is anticipated that new self-care methods made possible by technological advancements in the digital realm will be adopted in the future. Continuous as well as flash glucose monitoring systems, for instance, allow the sensors to serve as display devices, without wires transmit glucose data to a reader or mobile application, and generate hypo- and hyper-glycemia alerts \[[14](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_014)\]. Eye-tracking can be used to guide patient-focused product development of new systems for drug delivery (self-injection systems) through the procedure of usability testing. The development could greatly improve how the various parts of the linked system like the sensors, pumps for infusion, handheld devices, and mobile apps interact with one another, increasing the product’s overall efficacy \[[15](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_015)\]. Cutting-edge technologies like virtual reality (VR) and augmented reality (AR) can raise the bar for medical and healthcare practices by enabling users to interact with and completely lose themselves in these worlds. These technologies include head-mounted displays, haptic devices, AR glasses, software, and tracking sensors. In plastic surgery, virtual reality (VR) has become popular and has potential uses in navigation, training, and surgical preparation. Training using VR has been well-studied. Currently, the market is filled with several commercial VR goods for medical education \[[16](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_016)\].

## 13.2 Future trends and innovation of AI in drug discovery

Drug development is still a lengthy, costly, ineffective, and difficult process with a low rate of new-effective therapeutic discovery, especially in light of the complexity of disorders. Researchers have overcome obstacles in drug discovery with the help of AI/ML-based techniques, and these approaches have a wide range of applications in drug discovery programs for different diseases. In general, researchers from academia and business have expressed a great deal of interest in the application of AI/ML to pharmaceutical development \[[17](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_017)\]. Furthermore, a large number of pharmaceutical companies have collaborated with colleges and universities and AI start-ups, as well as invested in their AI-based research initiatives. In reaction to the COVID-19 pandemic, academia and industry have recently shown an impressive level of cooperation. Due to open-sourced platforms, data and pipelines to AI labs and businesses can now be shared. For instance, the public can now view the 3D models of SARS-CoV-2 proteins that Google Deepmind’s AlphaFold system predicted. The application of AI technology to enhance drug discovery is still in its infancy, even though AI has become a vital instrument for revolutionizing the medical development process \[[18](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_018)\]. Because most next-generation AI structures are “black boxes,” finding new drugs becomes more challenging. Because AI/ML results are difficult to interpret, their applications are restricted. It isn’t true for ML models that are simpler (like XGBoost, TensorFlow, Lasso, Ridge, and Elastic Net) or more complex (like DNN). As a result, scientists are unable to understand the biological processes that underlie the model’s output. Further complicating troubleshooting is the unpredictable malfunctions of these models. As a result, creating strategies for unlocking the DL black boxes is crucial \[[19](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_019)\].

Furthermore, since sufficient huge quantities of data to train them are necessary for the successful development of algorithms, the amount of data that is available directly impacts the effectiveness of AI/ML models. Finding novel targets or medications to treat a variety of neurological disorders for which there is no known treatment presents a unique challenge. Transfer learning technology, which applies knowledge from one task to another, can address the lack of training examples for a given drug discovery task. However, information sharing within the field of science appears to be the most promising long-term solution to data scarcity \[[20](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_020)\]. The process of developing drugs would greatly benefit from such widespread data sharing, and shortly, faster devices could result from hardware advancements like quantum computing. The difficulty in identifying targets is a particular limitation of using AI/ML for drug discovery, as many neurological disorders have unclear pathophysiology. More sophisticated AI and ML tools that can extract information from data sets created across the different biological layers – such as transcriptomics, proteomics, and metabolomics – are required to study the intricate disease mechanisms and identify the appropriate biological targets \[[21](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_021)\]. Capsule networks are a futuristic AI construction that can help in the situation. It is a networked module that houses CNNs. The remarkable ability of capsule networks to predict the cardiovascular side effects of materials during initial application highlights their unique potential in drug discovery efforts. Due to CNNs’ modular design, capsule neural networks can learn from a variety of data sets while maintaining the data’s ordered features. Because illness records have a highly adaptable structure, capsule networks can be used to study the way various layers of protein molecules, metabolites, and genes connect and shift in function concerning different diseases \[[22](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_022)\].

The resurgence of AI in organic synthesis can be attributed to developments in big data, computational power, deep neural networks, and optimization algorithms. Their primary drawback is their dependence on unambiguous chemical changes and reactions \[[23](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_023), [24](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_024), [25](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_025)\]. These have to be built and assembled manually. Transformer depictions and sequence-to-sequence models are two methods for natural language processing that have recently gained traction in the area. The line of investigation is driven by the discovery that the distribution sequence of organic molecule fragments is comparable to the English word sequence. When rule-free approaches are applied, the products are usually conceived as part of a text-based representation. After processing the products, encoder–decoder architecture is used to predict their corresponding artificial precursors at a single-step response distance \[[26](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_026)\]. Moreover, data indicates that the application of AI to the research and development of pharmaceuticals is beginning to gain traction. These methods are gradually living up to some of the community’s expectations and are demonstrating remarkable advancements in synthesis planning, de novo molecule design, and quantitative structure-activity relationship (QSAR) modeling, among other fields. It’s unclear, though; if these methods will help scientists create and synthesize “better drugs more quickly”. Techniques based on more “raw” chemical pictures (e.g., neural networks with graphs and SMILES-based recurrent neural networks) ought to work at least as well as standard descriptor-based models in the context of ligand-based property prediction. Furthermore, a greater range of chemical materials and modeling tasks can be easily applied with deep learning techniques, enabling more effective data utilization through online and multitask learning. Presently, conformation-aware deep learning is in its infancy, particularly when approaches that make use of three-dimensional imbalances in their construction are taken into account. Nevertheless, they should advance quickly in the field of drug discovery as well as related fields like material science and quantum mechanics, especially when used to replace more computationally intensive first-principle calculations \[[27](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_027), [28](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_028)\].

Using generative models and deep learning, scientists can produce novel substances with target-binding properties that increase drug efficacy and decrease side effects. With AI, individualized dose formulations for each patient will be feasible. AI algorithms will take into account patient-specific factors like age, weight, genetics, and illness status to optimize drug pieces and delivery techniques and improve treatment outcomes. The application of AI algorithms to predict the toxicity and side effects of suggested medications will revolutionize safety evaluation. Medication adherence and remote patient care will be made possible by AI-powered monitoring systems. Wearable technology and instruments will make it easier to collect data continuously, which will allow AI algorithms to create more individualized treatment plans and improve adherence. AI enhances patient recruitment, patient selection, and clinical trial design. AI algorithms will identify eligible patients, reduce trial costs, and expedite approval by utilizing genetic profiles, biomarkers, and electronic health records \[[29](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_029), [30](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_030)\]. Continuous manufacturing operations will be optimized by AI models’ real-time monitoring and oversight of critical variables. Through data analysis and feedback, AI algorithms will make pharmaceutical manufacturing consistent and effective. Massive data sets will be analyzed by AI to support regulatory decision-making. It will expedite the approval process for pharmaceuticals by regulatory agencies and enhance safety. More creative approaches and procedures must be employed since the creation and study of pharmaceuticals necessitates such a significant time and financial commitment \[[31](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_031)\]. The medical field is seeing significant opportunities due to AI. These include extensive multivariate data analysis; complex problem-solving in the development of workable medication delivery systems; enhanced decision-making; disease classification; dosage ratio optimization; quick drug development; prediction of drug reactions and bioactivities; cellular response; the efficacy of combination medications; and a host of other topics. As each section has shown, AI and machine learning have enormous potential to transform the way that medications are administered and improve the efficacy of therapies for infectious diseases \[[32](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_032)\].

## 13.3 Future trends and innovations of AI in drug manufacturing

Initially, AI-based manufacturing control methods were limited to services for which official or analytical structures were lacking because of the complexity of the processes involved or the high computational costs associated with developing adjustable process controllers. However, like other factory groups, these kinds of applications will continue to be made possible by the growing number of instruments, the capacity to gather enormous amounts of data, and the ability to extract crucial process signals and features. These applications do not call for an exact mathematical model or a structured physics-based demonstration of the system \[[33](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_033), [34](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_034)\]. Factories are getting smarter as companies utilize AI intensively to transform data from various production system components into useful insights. However, a lot of gaps still need to be closed to ensure that integrating AI into manufacturing processes is simple.

At every rung of the manufacturing chain hierarchy, ML has been employed exclusively. However, ML is not as popular at the system-wide level for decision-making as it once was, especially when compared to its successes in more specialized applications such as process monitoring, optimization, and population health management (PHM). The main reasons for this are the complex multi-stage processes, the stochastic and nonlinear dynamic characteristics of these processes, and the dependencies among the enormous amounts of diverse information generated in manufacturing systems. Furthermore, even though ML has the potential to solve non-deterministic polynomial (NP)-complete problems, which are frequently present in intelligent optimization problems, it is still challenging to select the best techniques and algorithms. A complete understanding of the problem, its root causes, consequences, and the intended solution must be known or thoroughly researched to increase the likelihood of choosing an AI tool that will be efficient and facilitate the ensuing creation of models, data analysis, and interpretation. The root cause of all these issues is the requirement for sufficient domain knowledge during the problem definition phase. It is required to make sure that every facet of the issue is comprehended and that no important information or suppositions are missed. Numerous interrelated plant management structures that control the plant’s overall performance are positioned at the top of the manufacturing hierarchy. No single AI tool, or even a combination of tools, has been capable to bridge and integrate any of the performance goals of these control systems, despite the development of standalone machine learning approaches. A technique known as reinforcement learning (RL) has been suggested \[[35](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_035)\]. Furthermore, because it would be excessively costly and labor-intensive to gather data in an organized way from different manufacturing configurations as well as operating conditions, real-world applications typically find it impractical to do so. Furthermore, limitations on the exchange of information and accessibility via both private and public networks may result from security concerns. To address these challenges, the field of science has investigated two strategies: data synthesis and transfer learning. The process of transferring an existing model using a range of methods from an initial data-rich domain of origin to an education data-poor target domain is known as transfer learning. The most well-known advantage of transferable learning for fault diagnosis prognosis and condition monitoring is that it allows experimental data from defect-free machines to be used for building models on real production lines if faulty conditions occur \[[36](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_036)\].

One application of transfer learning is the recognition of human actions in the context of human-robot cooperation during assembly. The example shows how transfer learning can address model scalability through appropriate transformations that match particular manufacturing scenarios, potentially leading to the establishment of a general solution instead of a specific one for a particular manufacturing aspect. However, it is also observed that transfer learning is still restricted to ad hoc, trial-and-error methods at this point \[[37](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_037)\]. To ensure that transfer learning-based approaches are widely applicable and accepted, more thorough theoretical research on subjects like data transferability is still necessary. By learning enough characteristics of the real data, we can create synthetic data that closely mimics the real data, allowing us to get around the restriction of data availability. The process is known as data synthesis \[[38](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_038)\]. Conventional data synthesis methods, like the Synthetic Minority Over-sampling Technique (SMOTE), often rely on interpolation, which is insufficient to capture complex features in the data. Generative adversarial networks (GANs) is a deep learning technique that made significant strides in synthesizing high-fidelity data and learning salient features. The fundamental idea behind a GAN is a competition between two components: a discriminator that separates synthetic data from real data and a generator that uses real data analysis to create synthetic data. There have been reports on early GAN implementation efforts \[[39](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_039)\]. For instance, to develop the gearbox fault diagnostic model, researchers looked into the use of synthetic vibration signals. According to spectral analysis, the synthetic data successfully captured the distinctive frequencies and other fault-related signal features. Similar to other deep learning methods, a comprehensive analysis of the underlying theories of GAN is still required to demonstrate the technique’s applicability in resolving data limitation issues \[[40](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_040)\].

There are new challenges arising from the boosting availability of various data in manufacturing systems. First, there’s a chance that a significant portion of the data is either completely missing or contains a lot of unnecessary and irrelevant information. Because the accuracy and availability of manufacturing data have a significant impact on the suitability and efficacy of AI algorithms about expected outcomes, these information curation issues pose a challenge to the practical application of ML algorithms. Second, the setting that is created throughout the data gathering and structuring process determines the quality of any information that can be gleaned from data analysis. Gathering and arranging data techniques must dynamically link various data sets, and information, along with models to take into account multiple viewpoints for multiple uses, given the wide range of viewpoints in production. Prospects for aligned local correlation-based forecasts (e.g., tool wellness and person process status) might be made possible by improved data quality. It will enable a causal understanding of the intricate and dynamic manufacturing processes that occur in factories \[[41](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_041)\].

Given the restrictions on the use of contemporary technology that affect the decisions that are made about people’s lives, it may be difficult to replace every antiquated or traditional scientific technique that is part of the healthcare infrastructure of today. These issues won’t be resolved until every element of the current infrastructure – including the medical personnel – supports improving the situation and the available resources. Furthermore, it is crucial to comprehend the potential benefits and drawbacks of utilizing the technology, which may require modifications to the current infrastructure. Ultimately, it would require a large amount of budgetary and financial support from the organization in charge of overseeing healthcare infrastructure. Even though there are certain limitations on the use of contemporary technology that would place those in charge of making crucial decisions about people’s lives in a difficult position, there might be difficulties in replacing all of the antiquated or traditional technical techniques that are employed in the current medical system \[[42](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_042)\]. In addition to their primary role in addressing current issues in their respective fields of study, AI and ML-based solutions can be implemented in several healthcare subfields. One such area, which contributes to the annual increase in global death rates, is the diagnosis of common illnesses and diseases. A significant portion of this issue can be resolved with the aid of deep neural networks and other AI-based technologies. With the rapid rate of population growth, doctors and other staff members would also find it challenging to complete their duties effectively due to excessive workloads and an absence of free time. As a result, hospitals can use ML and AI to help them solve the issue. These technologies can save critical time and resources by learning to predict every possible outcome at every stage with little room for error, which speeds up subsequent procedures. However, to carry out such solutions, hospitals might have to make investments in training physicians to use the technology, which requires the preparation of large datasets. Despite this, the existing infrastructure can incorporate virtual intelligence with minimal upfront costs for setting up internal diagnostic centers. It is anticipated that the long-term benefits will exceed those initial costs \[[43](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_043)\].

## 13.4 Future trends and innovations of AI in healthcare

AI in health research and initiatives has surged in the last few years, combining various clinical data elements, establishing links between bio repositories and clinical data, and creating links between clinical data and pharmaceutical research and development. More importantly, knowledge and data must be combined to create AI algorithms for health that work. Unlike other computational fields like sight and speech evaluation, where enormous amounts of information can be obtained, patient data is often sparse and subject to substantial variability \[[44](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_044), [45](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_045)\]. Furthermore, health issues in the real world are frequently complicated. Rule-based systems, for example, are examples of conventional AI technologies with high interpretability. Even though deep-learning models and other recent AI technologies may achieve beneficial quantitative performance, they are largely disregarded. Interpretability of models is essential and has been the topic of many conversations lately. The model interpretability may not always be as important as it might be, especially in scenarios where AI algorithms can produce accurate results consistently and widely. However, it isn’t the medical case – at most not with computer science currently dominating healthcare analytics \[[46](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_046), [47](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_047), [48](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_048)\]. For instance, it demonstrated that when employing claims data and electronic health records for readmission to hospital tasks, deep learning models were able to match the effectiveness of logistic regression. While deep-learning algorithms have demonstrated cutting-edge efficacy for medical image analysis, their generalizability remains questionable. In other words, it is difficult to argue that a model that performs well on a medical image data set from one radiology center will perform well on a data set from another radiology center. Additionally, human clinicians typically make decisions in healthcare, with AI algorithms serving as tools to assist them. To reassure the clinician, it is crucial to present comprehensive explanations for the assumptions made by those AI algorithms. Additionally, integrating AI algorithms into routine clinical workflows will enhance their clinical utility \[[49](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_049), [50](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_050)\].

While there are numerous AI models being developed for healthcare, there is a potential risk regarding their security. Researchers found that autonomous driving systems can be easily tricked by pollution on transportation signs. Researchers found that even small changes to a patient’s electronic health record can significantly change the predictor’s mortality estimate, even if the predictor is otherwise well-trained \[[51](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_051), [52](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_052), [53](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_053), [54](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_054)\]. A more thorough explanation of the possible worries regarding the “incentives for developed adversarial assaults” in the healthcare sector is given by another researcher. According to the authors of the study, (i) medical practitioners must understand the possible risk; (ii) AI researchers create strong defenses against medical adversarial assaults; and (iii) it is recommended that policymakers consider the possibility of model security risks when developing new regulatory frameworks. All AI models require samples of training data \[[55](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_055)\]. Generally, patient shifts and the intricate nature of their health issues are not well captured by a training sample size that is derived from patient data. A model created with patients from a single facility frequently does not work with patients from another. One of the main problems AI still has in the healthcare industry is the problem, also known as bias in the data. The researcher claims that bias of the following kind can exacerbate health disparities. The collection of extensive and varied patient data sets is one strategy to lessen bias \[[56](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_056), [57](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_057), [58](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_058)\].

Numerous prominent healthcare companies worldwide are involved in and have announced various ongoing and upcoming Metaverse projects as shown in [Fig. 13.2](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_fig_002). The Blockchain-Metaverse project HealthLand.io, also referred to as Healthify, was unveiled to offer its users virtual clubs, health excursions, meetings with heroes, and physical and mental health. Additionally, the project will enable patients and customers to use online stores and medical professionals to open online gyms. The idea is to establish a virtual community where people from all over the world come together for a common cause, irrespective of their nationality, religion, or ethnic background \[[59](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_059)\]. A decentralized Metaverse has been conceptualized by the British organization DeHealth as a way to simplify the healthcare ecosystem. Along with MR, the technology will expand VR and AR. With the help of the platform, patients and doctors will be able to collaborate virtually, communicate with one another, and make virtual income by selling their private medical records \[[60](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_060)\]. Bump Galaxy is a prototype Metaverse for mental health services built on Minecraft. Through the use of gaming environments, the project seeks to address patients’ mental health concerns. In addition to giving the patients a sense of safety and support from society, the “game world therapy” will help them get over trauma, depression, or anxiety. By using a bottom-up approach, the deep hypnotic visualizations will improve mental health and resilience within the community \[[61](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_061)\].

![Fig. 13.2: Schematic representation of various ongoing and upcoming Metaverse projects in healthcare companies.](graphic/converted/b_9783111574288-013_fig_002.png)

Fig. 13.2: Schematic representation of various ongoing and upcoming Metaverse projects in healthcare companies.

The vascular access rate will be greatly increased by the vein visualizing project AccuVein, which will be a very useful tool for physicians, neurosurgeons, and patients. It will raise the percentage of first-time injections that are successful and also raise the annual savings following clinical procedures. Following vein access, patients will not be concerned about bruises, and surgeons will be able to see a vast and intricate network of blood vessels. The project will undoubtedly enhance the security and caliber of treatments in critical medical applications \[[62](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_062)\]. A new Metaverse structure called HintVR was created by the California-based technological advances startup 8Chili to help physicians, surgeons, patients, clinical staff, paramedical workers, medical students, and material curators. Using cutting-edge 3D visualizations for image-guided surgeries, surgeons can also have creative and engaging interactions with patients before, during, and after surgery. The platform therefore has the potential to greatly assist the following parties: (i) physicians in making consultations more straightforward; (ii) surgeons in performing less invasive surgeries more skillfully; (iii) patients in understanding diagnosis, treatment options, and patient reviews; alongside (iv) medical/paramedical workers in competently adhering to all expert instructions \[[63](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_063)\]. The goal of the Dutch startup project HealthBlocks, which is based on the IoTex Blockchain, is to eradicate healthcare inequality worldwide. It can help the patient in addition to providing remote patient monitoring. A new, highly secure, private ecosystem will be created by smart devices with decentralized identities. Users from all over the world can change their daily unhealthy habits and adopt a healthy lifestyle by completing the Web3 Metaverse application and earning rewards. The project aims to provide reduced transaction costs, quicker transaction times, and increased scalability \[[64](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_064)\].

The development of a fully-connected society where smooth machine-to-machine along with machine-to-human communication anticipated is being fueled by the commercial launch of 5 G technology. The development of innovative biomaterials, intelligent materials, smart textiles, and sensors based on micro-electro-mechanical systems (MEMS), in addition to remote monitoring and illness diagnosis, is the focus of current research. Low-power, dependable, and safe network capabilities are needed for operating these sensor devices. Accurate real-time big data handling processing algorithms are required to handle the data collected by the sensors. The system’s overall performance can be assessed using its quality of service (QoS). Future directions for H-IoT research can be seen in the possibility of QoS improvements. Today’s technological state presents a plethora of opportunities for creativity and advancement. The scientific community has contributed to the development of several critical technologies that have made it possible for future healthcare services delivered via the H-IoT system \[[65](#9783111573861_018790.xhtml#b_9783111574288-013_ref_065)\].

## 13.5 Conclusion

Higher mental and intellectual capacities have replaced manual labor in many industries, including healthcare, due to AI. AI will be utilized in smarter hospitals and patient-physician interactions in the future of smart-connected healthcare. AI uses pertinent data sources to improve performance across various tasks. Technological innovations like VR alongside augmented reality (AR), which enable users to interact and immerse themselves fully in virtual environments, have the potential to raise the bar for medical and healthcare procedures. Initially, the application of AI techniques in manufacturing process control was limited to scenarios with a limited number of formal or analytical models. These applications are becoming simpler, though, due to the expansion of instrumentation, data collection, and the capacity to extract crucial process signals and features. Factories are getting smarter as AI is applied more and more in manufacturing processes. Making the best decisions about techniques and algorithms is still challenging. A comprehensive understanding of the problem, its causes, repercussions, and desired solution must be spoken or thoroughly researched to increase the likelihood of choosing an AI tool that will be effective and facilitate the processes of creating models, assessing data, and interpreting it. Numerous well-known healthcare organizations, including HealthLand.io, DeHealth, Bump Galaxy, AccuVein, HintVR, and HealthBlocks, have disclosed their current and future Metaverse initiatives. These initiatives seek to eliminate healthcare disparities, enhance mental health services, increase vascular access rates, and simplify the healthcare system. The commercial launch of 5 G technology fuels the development of novel biomaterials, intelligent materials, smart fabrics, and sensors based on MEMS, remote monitoring, and disease diagnosis. The QoS of these systems can be used to evaluate their overall performance. The state of technology today offers numerous opportunities for improvement and innovation, with future healthcare services provided via the H-IoT system becoming a reality due to the development of critical technologies that has helped the research community.
